Appendix A
Methodological extension:
Reg-NPMLE
for discrete
M_{i}

A natural generalization beyond
Reg-NPMLE
for discrete
M_{i}
is to consider a bin-based prior estimate for
\sigma_{i}^{2}\mid M_{i}
. In particular, we assume that those units with similar
M_{i}
value would also share similar
\sigma_{i}^{2}
. Instead of positing a prior on bivariate nuisances, we group units by the value of
M_{i}
, and estimate a separate prior for
\sigma_{i}^{2}
within each group. Let
b(i)\in\{1,...,B\}
denote the index of the bin to which unit
i
is assigned, where the binning is induced by
M_{i}
. In addition to (
15
), we further assume that within each bin
b
,

\sigma_{i}^{2}\mid b(i)=b\sim H_{b},\;\text{ and }\theta_{i}\text{ are deterministic}.

The corresponding oracle partially Bayes p-values that use the exact knowledge of
\{H_{b}\}
are defined as,

\displaystyle P_{i}^{\mathrm{djt}}=\frac{\int_{0}^{\infty}2\Phi(-\frac{|Z_{i}|}{\nu\sigma_{i}})p_{\chi^{2}}(S_{i}^{2}\mid K-p,\sigma_{i}^{2})H_{b(i)}(\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}^{2})}{\int_{0}^{\infty}p_{\chi^{2}}(S_{i}^{2}\mid K-p,\sigma_{i}^{2})H_{b(i)}(\mathrm{d}\sigma_{i}^{2})}\qquad\mbox{for $i\in[n]$.}

(S1)

For each bin
b
, we estimate
H_{b}
from the data within that bin by NPMLE using the marginal distribution of the statistics
\{S_{i}^{2}:b(i)=b\}
. In that direction, for any mixing measure
H^{\prime}_{b}
, define

f_{H_{b}^{\prime},K-p}(s^{2})=\int_{0}^{\infty}p_{\chi^{2}}(s^{2}\mid K-p,\sigma^{2})\,H_{b}^{\prime}(\mathrm{d}\sigma^{2}).

We can then estimate
H_{b}
by solving the following optimization problem

\displaystyle\widehat{H}_{b}\in\argmax_{H^{\prime}_{b}\in\mathcal{G}_{H_{B}}}\frac{1}{\#\{i:b(i)=b\}}\sum_{i:b(i)=b}\log f_{H^{\prime}_{b},K-p}(S^{2}_{i}),

(S2)

where
\mathcal{G}_{H_{B}}:=\{H^{\prime}_{b}:H^{\prime}_{b}(\underline{L},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U})=1\}
for the same absolute constants
\underline{L},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U}>0
used in (
25
). This problem can be solved in essentially the same way as in (
19
). Then a plug-in estimate
\widehat{P}^{\mathrm{djt}}_{i}
of the oracle p-values
P^{\mathrm{djt}}_{i}
are constructed by replacing each
H_{b}
with
\widehat{H}_{b}
in (
S1
). The full procedure is summarized in Algorithm
S1
.

Algorithm S1

limma-trend
generlization with discrete
M_{i}
(
Joint-NPMLE
)

1:

Partition the units into
B
bins according to the value of
M_{i}
, and construct bin index
b(i)
.

2:

Estimate
H_{b}
using
\widehat{H}_{b}
through NPMLE (cf. (
S2
)) for each
b=1,...,B
.

3:

Compute the p-values
\widehat{P}^{\mathrm{djt}}_{i}
using (
S1
) by replacing
H_{b(i)}
with
\widehat{H}_{b(i)}
.

Appendix B
Implementation details

B.1
Estimation of
\widehat{m}(\cdot)
in Algorithms
1
and
2

We adopt the trend estimation procedure from the implementation of
limma-trend
in
R

(
40
)
. We model
y=\log{(S_{i}^{2})}
as a smooth function of
A_{i}
using a natural cubic spline basis. The spline degrees of freedom are chosen adaptively according to the number of observations and capped by the number of distinct
A_{i}
values. In particular, let
n
be the number of units in
y
and
U_{A}
be the number of distinct observed
A_{i}
values. The spline degrees of freedom are chosen as

\nu=\min\left\{1+\mathbf{1}(n\geq 3)+\mathbf{1}(n\geq 6)+\mathbf{1}(n\geq 30),\ U_{A}\right\}.

If
\nu<2
, we use the constant fit
\widehat{m}(a)\equiv\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{y}
; otherwise, we fit a natural spline with
\nu
degrees of freedom by least squares and use the resulting fitted curve as
\widehat{m}(\cdot)
.

B.2
Discretization for NPMLE computation in
Reg-NPMLE

As observed before, the optimization program in (
19
) is convex. In our implementation, we use the interior point convex programming solver MOSEK
(
37
)
with the discretization technique proposed by
27
to solve it. In particular, we choose
B=300
grid points that are logarithmically spaced between the
1\%
quantile and the largest value of
\{\widehat{V}_{i}^{2},...,\widehat{V}_{n}^{2}\}
. We optimize (
19
) over all distributions supported on this finite grid. This is a conic programming problem and the solution is computed using MOSEK.

B.3
Discretization for NPMLE computation in
Joint-NPMLE

To compute (
25
), we restrict attention to distributions supported on a finite two-dimensional grid and optimize only over the corresponding probability masses. We adopt the following discretization scheme. We partition
A_{i}
into
B=50
bins, which are constructed deterministically from the order statistics of
A_{i}
, yielding
B
consecutive intervals with roughly equal counts.

For each bin
b
, let
I_{b}
be the set of indices in that bin and let
\mathcal{M}_{b}:=\{A_{i}:i\in I_{b}\}
be the set of observed
A_{i}
-values in the same bin. We use
\mathcal{M}_{b}
as the grid points for
\mu
. Next, letting
\widehat{m}(\cdot)
be the trend fitted in Algorithm
1
, we define the residuals
r_{i}:=\log S_{i}^{2}-\widehat{m}(A_{i})
for
i=1,...,n
and construct a common residual grid
\mathcal{R}_{p_{v}}=\{r^{(1)},...,r^{(p_{v})}\}
using
p_{v}=50
equally spaced points between
1\%
quantile and the maximum of
\{r_{1},...,r_{n}\}
. For each bin, let
u_{b}
be the median of
A_{i}
’s within the bin
b
. We define the bin-specific trend-centered grid for
\sigma^{2}
given by
\mathcal{V}_{b}=\{\exp{\left(\widehat{m}(u_{b})+r\right)}:r\in\mathcal{R}_{p_{v}}\}
.

For each pair
(b,v)
with
b\in[B]
and
v\in\mathcal{V}_{b}
, the bin-level likelihood contribution for each data unit
i
is defined as

L_{i,b,v}=p_{\chi^{2}}(S_{i}^{2}\mid K-p,\sigma^{2}=v)\cdot\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{b}|}\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{b}}\phi\left(A_{i};\mu,\frac{v}{K}\right),

where
\phi(x;\mu,\sigma^{2})
denotes the density of a univariate Gaussian distribution with mean
\mu
and variance
\sigma^{2}
evaluated at
x
, and optimize over probability masses
\{f_{b,v}\}
by solving

\max_{\{f_{b,v}\}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\left(\sum_{b=1}^{B}\sum_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{b}}f_{b,v}L_{i,b,v}\right),\quad f_{b,v}\geq 0,\quad\sum_{b=1}^{B}\sum_{v\in\mathcal{V}_{b}}f_{b,v}=1.

This is a finite-dimensional convex optimization problem that can be solved numerically using MOSEK. After optimization, each fitted bin-level mass
\widehat{f}_{b,v}
is mapped back to point masses on
(\mu,\sigma^{2})
by distributing each
\widehat{f}_{b,v}
equally over the atoms in
\mathcal{M}_{b}\times\{v\}
. That is, for every
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{b}
, we assign mass
\widehat{f}_{b,v}/|\mathcal{M}_{b}|
to the point
(\mu,v)
. Therefore, the final discretized estimator is supported on

\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{jt}}:=\bigcup_{b=1}^{B}\left\{(\mu,v):\,\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{b}\;\text{and}\;v\in\mathcal{V}_{b}\right\}.

Note that the choice of
B
, the size of the residual grid
p_{v}
, and the truncation quantiles for constructing the common residual grid
\mathcal{R}
are tuning parameters and can be adjusted to accommodate different datasets and computational budgets. We ignore the discretization error in the theoretical development.

B.4
Implementation for baseline methods in Section
5

•

Parametric
Limma
(Untrended-Inv
\chi^{2}
) and
Reg-NPMLE
.

As described in Section
2.1
and Section
2.2
, we will need to estimate
\kappa_{0}
and
s_{0}^{2}
in (
5
) and (
9
) for Untrended-Inv
\chi^{2}
and
Reg-NPMLE
respectively. Indeed, we use the methods of moments that is provided in
46
, and also the build in method in the
limma
package to estimate
(\kappa_{0},s_{0}^{2})
for both Untrended-Inv
\chi^{2}
and
Reg-NPMLE
. In particular, for Untrended-Inv
\chi^{2}
, we estimate it by the following procedure (denote
d=K-p
):

\displaystyle z_{i}

\displaystyle=\log{S_{i}^{2}},

\displaystyle e_{i}

\displaystyle=z_{i}-\psi(d/2)+\log{(d/2)},

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}(\widehat{\kappa}_{0}/2)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{mean}\{(e_{i}-\bar{e})^{2}n/(n-1)-\psi^{{}^{\prime}}(d/2)\},

\displaystyle\widehat{s}_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle=\exp{(\text{mean}(e)+\psi(\widehat{\kappa}_{0}/2)-\log(\widehat{\kappa}_{0}/2))},

where
\psi(\cdot)
and
\psi^{\prime}(\cdot)
is the diagamma function and trigamma function respectively. Similarly for
Reg-NPMLE
, we estimate it as follows:

\displaystyle z_{i}

\displaystyle=\log S_{i}^{2},

\displaystyle r_{i}

\displaystyle=z_{i}-\widehat{m}(A_{i}),

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}(\widehat{\kappa}_{0}/2)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{mean}\{(r_{i}-\bar{r})^{2}\}n/(n-\nu)-\psi^{\prime}(d/2),

\displaystyle\widehat{s}_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{\psi(\widehat{\kappa}_{0}/2)-\log(\widehat{\kappa}_{0}/2)-\psi(d/2)+\log(d/2)\right\},

where
\widehat{m}
is the trend function estimated in Supplement
B.1
, and
\nu
is the degrees of freedom of the spline that is used for estimating
\widehat{m}(\cdot)
.

•

Untrended-NPMLE.
We refer to
22
for the description of the method. The prior
G
is estimated with NPMLE using similar techniques in Supplement
B.2
, with the optimization function being replaced as

\widehat{G}_{\texttt{limma}{}}\in\argmax_{G\in\mathcal{G}_{\texttt{limma}{}}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\left\{\int_{0}^{\infty}p_{\chi^{2}}\!\left(S_{i}^{2}\mid K-p,\sigma^{2}\right)G(\mathrm{d}\sigma^{2})\right\},

where
\mathcal{G}_{\texttt{limma}{}}
is the nonparametric class of distributions considered in
22
, and we choose
B=300
optimization grid points that are logarithmically spaced between the
1\%
quantile and the largest value of
\{S_{1}^{2},...,S_{n}^{2}\}
.

•

MAnorm2
(
51
)
.
We use the built-in function provided by the R package
MAnorm2

(
51
)
. For the two-sample comparison setting of Example
3
, we first construct
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{Y}_{iA}
,
\widehat{S}_{iA}^{2}
,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{Y}_{iB}
,
\widehat{S}_{iB}^{2}
, where
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{Y}_{iA}
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{Y}_{iB}
denote the treated/control group means,
\widehat{S}_{iA}^{2}
and
\widehat{S}_{iB}^{2}
denote the treated/control group sample variances for unit
i
. The function
fitMeanVarCurve
estimates a mean-variance curve

\widehat{\xi}_{\mathrm{MA}}(\cdot)
from the pooled collection of above pairs
\{(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{Y}_{iA},\widehat{S}_{iA}^{2})\}_{i=1}^{n}
,
\{(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{Y}_{iB},\widehat{S}_{iB}^{2})\}_{i=1}^{n}
using robust gamma family local regression. MAnorm2 then estimates the prior degrees of freedom
d_{0}
by matching the empirical variability of the normalized within-group variances to its theoretical counterpart. Specifically, for
g\in\{A,B\}
, define

z_{ig}=\log\left\{\frac{\widehat{S}_{ig}^{2}}{\widehat{\xi}_{\mathrm{MA}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{Y}_{ig})}\right\},\quad\nu_{g}=K_{g}-1,\quad\text{and assume working prior }\frac{1}{\sigma_{ig}^{2}}\sim\frac{\chi_{d_{0}}^{2}}{\widehat{\xi}_{\mathrm{MA}}(\mu_{ig})d_{0}}.

Under the working scaled Inv
\chi^{2}
model,
z_{ig}
is approximately distributed as
\log F_{\nu_{g},d_{0}}
, so that

\mathrm{Var}(z_{ig})\approx\psi_{1}\left(\frac{\nu_{g}}{2}\right)+\psi_{1}\left(\frac{d_{0}}{2}\right),

where
\psi_{1}
is the trigamma function. Thus
estimatePriorDf
estimates
d_{0}
by matching the observed sample variances of
z_{iA}
and
z_{iB}
to this expression. After this, MAnorm2 forms the moderated variance and the corresponding statistics

\widetilde{S}_{i}^{2}=\frac{\widehat{d}_{0}\,\widehat{\xi}_{\mathrm{MA}}(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{Y}_{i,\mathrm{avg}})+(K_{A}+K_{B}-2)\widehat{S}_{i,\mathrm{pool}}^{2}}{\widehat{d}_{0}+K_{A}+K_{B}-2},\quad T_{i}^{\mathrm{MA}}=\frac{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{Y}_{iB}-\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{Y}_{iA}}{\sqrt{\left(K_{A}^{-1}+K_{B}^{-1}\right)\widetilde{S}_{i}^{2}}},

with
Y_{i,\mathrm{avg}}=(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{Y}_{iA}+\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{Y}_{iB})/2
and
\widehat{S}_{i,\mathrm{pool}}^{2}=\{(K_{A}-1)\widehat{S}_{iA}^{2}+(K_{B}-1)\widehat{S}_{iB}^{2}\}/(K_{A}+K_{B}-2)
, and then the p-value is constructed as

2\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{F}_{t_{\widehat{d}_{0}+K_{A}+K_{B}-2}}(|T_{i}^{\mathrm{MA}}|)
.

•

MAP
(
30
)
.
To facilitate the comparison, we replace the built-in trend estimation procedure of MAP with the procedure used in the other methods (described in Supplement
B.1
). With the estimated
\widehat{m}(\cdot)
, we calculate the p-value as
2\Phi(-|Z_{i}|/\{\nu\exp(\widehat{m}(M_{i}))\})
.

Appendix C
Additional numerical results in Section
5

This section provides additional numerical results that extend the first experiment in Section
5
. We use a balanced design with
K_{A}=K_{B}
, and take
K\in\{4,6,10,18\}
. We consider three choices for
G
: (i)
G=\delta_{1}
(referred to as
Dirac
), (ii)
G=10\times\mathrm{Inv}\chi^{2}_{10}
(
Scaled Inv
\chi^{2}
), and (iii)
G=0.5\,\delta_{1}+0.5\,\delta_{10}
(
Two-point
).
We also consider two choices of the mean–variance trend function
m(\cdot)
: (1) Type 1 (without mean–variance trend):
\exp{\left(m(\mu_{i})\right)}=1
, and (2) Type 2 (with mean–variance trend):
m(\mu_{i})=-4\,\mathrm{logistic}\,((\mu_{i}-16)/4)+12
, where
\mathrm{logistic}(y)=(1+e^{-y})^{-1}
.

Results are summarized in Figure
S1
. We omit the MAP results from this figure, as it does not control the FDR in any of the simulation settings considered. Since the simulations are based on a balanced design, MAnorm2 performs very similarly to Reg-Inv
\chi^{2}
throughout these settings. The upper panel corresponds to
m
coming from Type 1 and the lower panel from Type 2 (these types are defined above). For
m
belonging to Type 1, for
Dirac
and
Scaled Inv
\chi^{2}
priors, we observe that the FDR is controlled at the correct nominal level across all degrees of freedom. However, for the
Two-point
prior, the prior specification in
Reg-Inv
\chi^{2}
and
limma
is wrong, resulting in inflated FDR for
K-p=4
, as shown in Section
5
. Across all the settings, we observe that the partially Bayes methods outperform t-tests in terms of power, demonstrating the benefit of pooling information across units. The impact of prior misspecification on power is evident in the third column of the upper panel, where the NPMLE-based procedures outperform those depending on parametric priors. The best performance is given by
Joint-NPMLE
and NPMLE-based
limma
from
(
22
)
, whereas
Reg-NPMLE
slightly suffers due to the trend estimation error. Since there is no trend in the oracle model,
Joint-NPMLE
does not exhibit an advantage. As the degrees of freedom increase, the performance of all the methods becomes comparable. Next, when
m
comes from Type 2 with non-constant mean-variance trend, the FDR of all the procedures behaves similarly as the previous setting. However, in this framework,
Reg-NPMLE
and
Joint-NPMLE
outperform all methods ignoring the mean variance trend. Furthermore, the performance of
Reg-NPMLE
is slightly worse compared to
Joint-NPMLE
. Again, the performance gap disappears as
K
increases.

(b) Type 2 (with mean-variance trend):

.

Appendix D
Additional details and results for real data examples

D.1
Preprocessing of the count data in Section
6.1

We downloaded the raw data from the Gene Expression Omnibus (GEO,
http://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/
) with accession number
GSE114716
. The raw data consist of a feature-by-sample count matrix, where each entry records the number of sequencing reads mapped to a given feature in a given sample. We conduct the following preprocessing steps using the edgeR
(
8
)
pipeline: (1) removed lowly expressed genes in a design-aware manner, (2) normalized for differences in library composition across samples using the trimmed mean of M-values (TMM) procedure, and (3) transformed the normalized counts to
\log_{2}
counts per million with a prior count of
3
for downstream analysis.

D.2
Details and diagnostic figure for proteomics application of Section
6.3

For the proteomics data in Section
6.3
,
Joint-NPMLE
is not directly applicable since the summary statistics are discrete. However, we can estimate the prior for
\sigma_{i}^{2}\mid M_{i}
using the bin based procedure in Supplement
A
.
In that direction, we stratify the proteins by peptide counts
M_{i}
, using exact strata for
M_{i}\in\{1,2,...,11\}
, and pooled
7
bins for larger
M_{i}
to ensure stable estimation of the variance prior.
Observe that the binning already takes care of the stratification, and hence, trend estimation is not required here.
The reversal across
M
in the number of discoveries made by the trended and the untrended methods, as observed in Figure
4
(c) can be understood as a consequence of variance misspecification under untrended modeling. Untrended methods shrink all units toward a common global variance trend, which may be smaller than the local variance level in low-
M
bins, leading to small estimated variances and therefore more significant findings. This phenomenon reverses as
M
increases. However, trend-based methods gain advantages from modeling the mean-variance relationship and thus avoid the corresponding misspecification. Figure
4
(d) displays the estimated prior on
\sigma_{i}^{2}
within each bin of
M
. The estimated discrete priors exhibit clear variation across bins in both support and mass allocation, indicating substantial heterogeneity in the variance distribution. To further assess model fit, we examine bin-wise marginal diagnostic plots (cf. Figure
S2
) for
S_{i}^{2}
, with particular focus on bins corresponding to small
M_{i}
. Within each bin, we compare the empirical distribution of the sample variances
S_{i}^{2}
with the model-implied marginal distribution obtained by
Reg-NPMLE
and discrete
Joint-NPMLE
. In bins with small
M_{i}
, the marginal distribution implied by
Reg-NPMLE
does not adequately match the observed distribution of
S_{i}^{2}
, with noticeable discrepancies in both shape and spread. In contrast, the marginal distribution implied by discrete
Joint-NPMLE
is more closely aligned. This supports the interpretation in the main text that, when
M_{i}
is small, a single smooth trend may be insufficient to capture the full conditional distribution of variances, whereas the bin-specific prior used by discrete
Joint-NPMLE
provides a more flexible and better-calibrated fit.

Figure S2
:
Marginal plots of the log sample variances
\log(S_{i}^{2})
for the proteomics data
57
, shown separately for subsets with
M_{i}\in\{1,\dots,6\}
. In each panel, the histograms of the log sample variance
\log(S_{i}^{2})
, is overlaid with the corresponding fitted marginal densities. We use 3 choices of estimated
G=\widehat{G}
for marginalization corresponding to Untrended-Inv
\chi^{2}
(in
red
),
Reg-NPMLE
(in
blue
), and
Joint-NPMLE
obtained by the bin-based procedure (in
blue
).

D.3
Differential expression of
Plasmodium falciparum
genes between severe and
non-severe malaria infections

Here, we analyze a bulk RNA-seq dataset sourced from
49
consisting of dual RNA-seq samples in which sequencing reads were aligned to both the human genome and the Plasmodium falciparum 3D7 reference genome. The primary unit of analysis is the parasite gene, and reads were allocated to parasite gene features using the P. falciparum 3D7 genome annotation, so that each count represents transcriptional evidence for a particular parasite gene in a given blood sample. We follow the pre-processing steps in
49
, and we obtain
n=4,144
genes for differential analysis. The main contrast of interest is the difference in parasite gene expression between severe and non-severe infections among
K=35
samples. The design matrix includes an additional covariate for the estimated parasite life-cycle stage proportion (e.g., ring stage) to adjust for between-sample differences in stage composition, which otherwise can confound severity-associated differential expression, therefore resulting in
p=3
covariates. Assumption
1
holds in this data even when the ring stage covariate is continuous. We apply Benjamini-Hochberg to control the FDR at
\alpha=0.05
after computing p-values with all
6
methods from Section
6.1
.

The results of significance are shown in Table
S1
, and Figure
S3
presents the same diagnostic plots as in Section
6.1
. Untrended-Inv
\chi^{2}
produces substantially more discoveries than the Untrended-NPMLE and the methods that incorporate a mean–variance trend. This inflation is plausibly driven by model misspecification. In particular, Figure
S3
(a) indicates that the marginal distribution of
S_{i}^{2}
implied by the parametric prior of Untrended-Inv
\chi^{2}
is noticeably misaligned with the empirical histogram, suggesting that this parametric fit does not adequately represent the variance distribution in the data, and may lead to more false discoveries.

Appendix E
Mathematical notation

We shall adopt the following notations to simplify the presentation of our mathematical arguments in the subsequent discussion. For two sequences
\{a_{n}\}
and
\{b_{n}\}
, we shall say that
a_{n}\lesssim_{\square}b_{n}
if there exists constants
C_{1}>0
(depending on the quantities expressed in
\square
but independent of
n
) such that
a_{n}\leq C_{1}b_{n}
, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Similarly, the notation
a_{n}\asymp_{\square}b_{n}
shall mean that there exists constants
C_{2},C_{3}>0
(depending on the quantities expressed in
\square
but independent of
n
) such that
C_{2}b_{n}\leq a_{n}\leq C_{3}b_{n}
, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. If there are no squares, then we just mean that the constants involved in the inequalities are independent of
n
but do not stress the dependence of such constants on other problem parameters. The sequence
a_{n}=o(b_{n})
, if
a_{n}/b_{n}\rightarrow 0
, as
n\rightarrow\infty
and
a_{n}=\omega(b_{n})
is
a_{n}/b_{n}\rightarrow\infty
, as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Furthermore, we shall denote the set of null indices by
\mathcal{H}_{0}:=\{i:\theta_{i}=0\}
.

Appendix F
Proofs of results in Section
2

F.1
Proof of Proposition
2

From the properties of ordinary least squares estimators, we can express

Z_{i}=c^{\top}_{\theta}(X^{\top}X)^{-1}X^{\top}Y_{i},\quad\widetilde{A}_{i}=c^{\top}_{\widetilde{A}}(X^{\top}X)^{-1}X^{\top}Y_{i},\quad S^{2}_{i}=Y^{\top}_{i}(I-P_{X})Y_{i}/(K-p),

where
Y_{i}=(Y_{i1},\ldots,Y_{iK})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
and
P_{X}
is the orthogonal projection matrix onto
\mathcal{C}(X)
, the column space of
X
. Since, conditioned on
(\beta_{i},\sigma^{2}_{i})
, the random vector
Y_{i}
is Gaussian for all
i\in[n]
, to conclude the proposition, it enough to conclude that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}(Z_{i},\widetilde{A}_{i})=0,\quad\mathrm{Cov}(Z_{i},(I-P_{X})Y_{i})=0,\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathrm{Cov}(\widetilde{A}_{i},(I-P_{X})Y_{i})=0,\quad\mbox{for all $i\in[n]$.}

(S3)

It is easy to observe that the above conditions translate to

\displaystyle c^{\top}_{\theta}(X^{\top}X)^{-1}c_{\widetilde{A}}=0,\quad c^{\top}_{\theta}(X^{\top}X)^{-1}X^{\top}(I-P_{X})=0,\quad\mbox{and}\quad c^{\top}_{\widetilde{A}}(X^{\top}X)^{-1}X^{\top}(I-P_{X})=0.

(S4)

The last two assertions always hold and the first condition is implied by Assumption
1
. Hence the proposition follows.

F.2
Proof of Example
4

Observe that since intercept is included in each of the fitted regression, therefore
\mathds{1}_{K}\in\mathcal{C}(X)
. Consequently, we have

(X^{\top}X)^{-1}c_{A}=\frac{1}{K}(X^{\top}X)^{-1}X^{\top}\mathds{1}_{K}=\frac{1}{K}(X^{\top}X)^{-1}X^{\top}Xe_{1}=\frac{1}{K}e_{1},

where
e_{1}=(1,0,\ldots,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{q+2}
. Furthermore, since
\tau_{i}
is not the intercept coefficient,
c^{\top}e_{1}=0
, which concludes the assertion in the example.

Appendix G
Properties of chi-square mixtures

Consider the following properties of mixtures of
\chi^{2}_{K-p}/(K-p)
densities defined in (
18
).

Lemma S1
.

For any mixing measure
G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{tr}}
, the densities
f_{G^{\prime},K-p}
defined in (
18
) satisfy the following properties.

1.

The derivative of
f_{G^{\prime},K-p}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})
with respect to
\eta\in(\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,2\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\mathsf{tr}})
is given by

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{G^{\prime},K-p}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})}{\partial\eta}

\displaystyle=\int C_{K,p}\,\exp\left(-\frac{(K-p)s^{2}}{2\eta^{2}\tau^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{2}\tau^{2}}\right)^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}

(S5)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}\left(\frac{2-K+p}{\eta}+\frac{(K-p)s^{2}}{\tau^{2}\eta^{3}}\right)\,\mathrm{d}G^{\prime}(\tau^{2}),

(S6)

where
C_{K,p}=(K-p)^{(K-p)/2}/(2^{(K-p)/2}\Gamma((K-p)/2))
. Hence

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}\log f_{G^{\prime},K-p}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})=\mathbb{E}_{\tau^{2}\sim G^{\prime}}\left[\frac{2-K+p}{\eta}+\frac{(K-p)X}{\tau^{2}\eta}\,\Big|\,X=\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right],

(S7)

where
X\sim\tau^{2}\chi^{2}_{K-p}/(K-p)
(we shall use the same notation in the rest of the lemma).

2.

The second derivative of
f_{G^{\prime},K-p}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})
with respect to
\eta
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\eta^{2}}\log f_{G^{\prime},K-p}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Var}_{\tau^{2}\sim G^{\prime}}\left[\frac{2-K+p}{\eta}+\frac{(K-p)X}{\tau^{2}\eta}\,\Big|\,X=\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right]

(S8)

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt-\mathbb{E}_{\tau^{2}\sim G^{\prime}}\left[\frac{2-K+p}{\eta^{2}}+\frac{3(K-p)X}{\tau^{2}\eta^{2}}\,\Big|\,X=\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right],

(S9)

for any
\eta\in(\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,2\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\mathsf{tr}})
.

3.

Recall
V^{2}_{i}
for
i=1,\ldots,n
defined as
V_{i}^{2}=S^{2}_{i}/\xi_{0}^{2}(M_{i})\sim\tau_{i}^{2}\chi^{2}_{K-p}/(K-p)
. If
K>p+2
and the parameters
\tau_{1}^{2},\ldots,\tau_{n}^{2}\in[\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}]
, then there exists
\underline{\kappa}>0
such that

\displaystyle\max_{i\leq n}\mathbb{P}\left(V_{i}^{2}/\tau_{i}^{2}\leq\underline{\kappa}\,n^{-6/(K-p)}\mid\tau^{2}_{i}\right)\leq n^{-3}.

(S10)

Similarly, there exists
\overline{\kappa}>0
such that

\displaystyle\max_{i\leq n}\mathbb{P}\left(V_{i}^{2}/\tau_{i}^{2}\geq 3\overline{\kappa}\log n\mid\tau^{2}_{i}\right)\leq n^{-3}.

(S11)

Furthermore, the constants
\underline{\kappa}
and
\overline{\kappa}
depend only on
K,p,\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\mathsf{tr}},\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}
. Similar results also hold for
S^{2}_{i}
with possibly different constants.

4.

There exists
\widetilde{\kappa}>0
, depending only on
K,p,\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\mathsf{tr}},\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{L}_{\mathsf{tr}}
, such that with
C_{n}=\widetilde{\kappa}\log n
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in(\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,\,2\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\mathsf{tr}})}\left|\int_{C_{n}}^{\infty}\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}f_{G^{\prime},K-p}\!\left(\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\,\mathrm{d}s^{2}\right|\lesssim_{K,p,\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\mathsf{tr}},\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}}\frac{1}{n^{2}}.

(S12)

While we state the above lemma for scale mixtures of
\chi^{2}_{K-p}
random variables, similar results continue to hold for scale mixtures of
\chi^{2}_{r}
random variables for any degrees of freedom
r>2
. Furthermore, (3) also holds for
V_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2}
for
i=1,\ldots,n
defined in Section
3.2
, if Assumptions
13
and
14
holds.

Proof.

Recall

p_{\chi^{2}}(x\mid K-p,\tau^{2})=C_{K,p}\exp\!\left(-\frac{(K-p)x}{2\tau^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{x}{\tau^{2}}\right)^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}\frac{1}{\tau^{2}},\qquad C_{K,p}=\frac{(K-p)^{(K-p)/2}}{2^{(K-p)/2}\Gamma((K-p)/2)},

and for any
x>0
, the mixture density is

f_{G,K-p}(x)=\int_{0}^{\infty}p_{\chi^{2}}(x\mid K-p,\tau^{2})\,G(\mathrm{d}\tau^{2}).

Fix
s^{2}>0
and
\eta\in(\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\mathsf{tr}})
and set
x(\eta)=s^{2}/\eta^{2}
.

(1) First derivative.

Define
x(\eta)=s^{2}/\eta^{2}
. We compute
\partial_{\eta}f_{G,K-p}(x(\eta))
by differentiating under the integral sign. For fixed
\tau^{2}
,

\log p_{\chi^{2}}(x(\eta)\mid K-p,\tau^{2})=\log C_{K,p}-\frac{(K-p)x(\eta)}{2\tau^{2}}+\Big(\frac{K-p}{2}-1\Big)\log x(\eta)-\frac{K-p}{2}\log\tau^{2}

By definition, we have
x^{\prime}(\eta)=-2s^{2}/\eta^{3}
and

\frac{d}{d\eta}\log x(\eta)=-\frac{2}{\eta}.

Hence

\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}\log p_{\chi^{2}}(x(\eta)\mid K-p,\tau^{2})=\frac{(K-p)s^{2}}{\tau^{2}\eta^{3}}+\frac{2-K+p}{\eta}.

Therefore

\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}p_{\chi^{2}}(x(\eta)\mid K-p,\tau^{2})=p_{\chi^{2}}(x(\eta)\mid K-p,\tau^{2})\left(\frac{2-K+p}{\eta}+\frac{(K-p)s^{2}}{\tau^{2}\eta^{3}}\right).

Assumption
6
implies
\tau^{2}\in[\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}]
almost surely under
G
, so the derivative is dominated by an integrable function of
\tau^{2}
. Differentiation under the integral sign is therefore justified, yielding

\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}f_{G,K-p}(x(\eta))=\int p_{\chi^{2}}(x(\eta)\mid K-p,\tau^{2})\left(\frac{2-K+p}{\eta}+\frac{(K-p)s^{2}}{\tau^{2}\eta^{3}}\right)\,G(\mathrm{d}\tau^{2}).

Dividing by
f_{G,K-p}(x(\eta))
yields the expression in (
S7
).

(2) Second derivative.

From (
S7
),

\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}\log f_{G,K-p}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{2-K+p}{\eta}+\frac{(K-p)X}{\tau^{2}\eta}\,\Big|\,X=\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right].

Differentiating once more and using dominated convergence yields

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\eta^{2}}\log f_{G,K-p}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{2-K+p}{\eta}+\frac{(K-p)X}{\tau^{2}\eta}\,\Big|\,X=\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{2-K+p}{\eta^{2}}+\frac{3(K-p)X}{\tau^{2}\eta^{2}}\,\Big|\,X=\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right],

which is exactly the identity stated in the lemma.

(3) Tail bounds.

For each
i
,

(V_{i}^{2}/\tau_{i}^{2})\mid\tau^{2}_{i}\sim\frac{\chi^{2}_{K-p}}{K-p}.

Thus it suffices to bound the lower and upper tails of
\chi^{2}_{K-p}
. For the lower tail observe that, for
0<t\leq 1
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\chi^{2}_{K-p}}{K-p}\leq t\right)\leq\frac{c_{K,p}}{2^{(K-p)/2}\Gamma((K-p)/2)}\int_{0}^{t}u^{(K-p)/2-1}\,\mathrm{d}u=c_{K,p}\,t^{(K-p)/2},

for some constant
c_{K,p}>0
.
Choosing
\underline{\kappa}>0
sufficiently small (depending on
K,p,\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}}
) such that
A_{n}=\underline{\kappa}n^{-6/(K-p)}\leq 1
and
c_{K,p}A_{n}^{(K-p)/2}\leq n^{-3}
yields

\max_{i\leq n}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{V_{i}^{2}}{\tau_{i}^{2}}\leq A_{n}\mid\tau^{2}_{i}\right)\leq n^{-3}.

Similarly, for the upper tail, for
t\geq K-p
, a Chernoff bound gives

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\chi^{2}_{K-p}}{K-p}\geq t\right)\leq\widetilde{c}_{K,p}\exp\!\left(-\frac{t}{4}+\frac{K-p}{2}\log 2\right),

for some constant
\widetilde{c}_{K,p}>0
.
Choosing
\overline{\kappa}>0
large enough (depending on
K,p,\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}
) such that
B_{n}=3\overline{\kappa}\log n\geq K-p
and
the above bound is at most
n^{-3}
yields

\max_{i\leq n}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{V_{i}^{2}}{\tau_{i}^{2}}\geq B_{n}\mid\tau^{2}_{i}\right)\leq n^{-3}.

Analogous results follow for
S^{2}_{i}
by noting that
V^{2}_{i}=S^{2}_{i}/\xi^{2}_{0}(A_{i})
and
\xi_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
defined in Assumption
6
.

(5) Tail bound for the integral of the score.

Using the derivative expression from part (1),

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}f_{G,K-p}\!\left(\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\int C_{K,p}\exp\!\left(-\frac{(K-p)s^{2}}{2\eta^{2}\tau^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{2}\tau^{2}}\right)^{(K-p)/2-1}

(S13)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}\left(\frac{2-K+p}{\eta}+\frac{(K-p)s^{2}}{\tau^{2}\eta^{3}}\right)\,G(\mathrm{d}\tau^{2}).

(S14)

Since
\eta\in(\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,2\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\mathsf{tr}})
and
\tau^{2}\in[\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}]
, there exist constants
c,C>0
depending only on
K,p,\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\mathsf{tr}},\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}
such that

\sup_{\eta}\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}f_{G,K-p}\!\left(\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\right|\leq Ce^{-cs^{2}}\{(s^{2})^{(K-p)/2-1}+(s^{2})^{(K-p)/2}\}.

Integrating over
[C_{n},\infty)
gives

\sup_{\eta}\left|\int_{C_{n}}^{\infty}\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}f_{G,K-p}\!\left(\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}s^{2}\right|\leq C\int_{C_{n}}^{\infty}e^{-cu}(u^{(K-p)/2-1}+u^{(K-p)/2})\mathrm{d}u.

Using the standard incomplete gamma bound,

\int_{t}^{\infty}u^{\alpha}e^{-cu}\mathrm{d}u\lesssim_{\alpha,c}e^{-ct}(1+t^{\alpha}),

and taking
C_{n}=\widetilde{\kappa}\log n
yields

\sup_{\eta}\left|\int_{C_{n}}^{\infty}\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}f_{G,K-p}\!\left(\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}s^{2}\right|\lesssim e^{-c\widetilde{\kappa}\log n}(1+(\widetilde{\kappa}\log n)^{(K-p)/2}).

Choosing
\widetilde{\kappa}
sufficiently large gives the bound
O(n^{-2})
, proving (
S12
).
∎

Next, consider the following result.

Lemma S2
.

Let

\displaystyle\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{\mathsf{tr}}=\Big\{f_{G,K-p}:\operatorname{supp}(G)\subset[\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}]\Big\},

(S15)

where
f_{G,K-p}
6
6

6

In this lemma and the subsequent proof, we shall occasionally abuse the notation slightly to use
f_{G}
for
f_{G,K-p}
.
is defined in (
18
). For
\eta\in[\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,\,2\overline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}]
, define the density and derivative semi-metrics on
(0,\infty)
by

\|f_{G_{1}}-f_{G_{2}}\|_{\infty,\eta}:=\sup_{s^{2}>0}\sup_{\eta\in[\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,\,2\overline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}]}\left|f_{G_{1},K-p}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})-f_{G_{2},K-p}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})\right|,

\|f_{G_{1}}-f_{G_{2}}\|_{\infty,\partial,\eta}:=\sup_{s^{2}>0}\sup_{\eta\in[\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,\,2\overline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}]}\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}f_{G_{1},K-p}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})-\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}f_{G_{2},K-p}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})\right|.

Let
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{joint}}:=\|\cdot\|_{\infty,\eta}\vee\|\cdot\|_{\infty,\partial,\eta}
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on
K,p,\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}},\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}
, such that for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\log N\!\left(\varepsilon,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{\mathsf{tr}},\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{joint}}\right)\leq C\bigl(\log(1/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{2}.

(S16)

Moreover, the same bound holds if the suprema over
s^{2}>0
in the definitions of
\|\cdot\|_{\infty,\eta}
and
\|\cdot\|_{\infty,\partial,\eta}
are replaced by suprema over any interval
[A_{n},B_{n}]
with
0<A_{n}<B_{n}<\infty
.

Proof.

For any semi-metrics
d_{1},d_{2}
on a class
\mathcal{F}
, the elementary product-cover bound yields

N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F},d_{1}\vee d_{2})\leq N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F},d_{1})\,N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F},d_{2}),

and hence

\log N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F},d_{1}\vee d_{2})\leq\log N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F},d_{1})+\log N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F},d_{2}).

We apply this with
\mathcal{F}=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{\mathsf{tr}}
,
d_{1}=\|\cdot\|_{\infty,\eta}
, and
d_{2}=\|\cdot\|_{\infty,\partial,\eta}
, which yields

\log N\!\left(\varepsilon,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{\mathsf{tr}},\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{joint}}\right)\leq\log N\!\left(\varepsilon,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{\mathsf{tr}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty,\eta}\right)+\log N\!\left(\varepsilon,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{\mathsf{tr}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty,\partial,\eta}\right).

By Lemma S.4 of
22
, there exists
C_{1}>0
depending only on the problem parameters
K,p,\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}},\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}
such that for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

\log N\!\left(\varepsilon,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{\mathsf{tr}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty,\eta}\right)\leq C_{1}\bigl(\log(1/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{2}.

Next, we consider the derivative term. For
\tau^{2}\in[\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}]
, differentiating under the integral sign gives

\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}f_{G}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})=\int\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}p_{\chi^{2}}(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\mid K-p,\tau^{2})\,G(\mathrm{d}\tau^{2}).

From Lemma
S1
, we can conclude that there exists a constant
C_{0}>0
depending only on the problem parameters
K,p,\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}},\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}
such that for all
s^{2}>0
,
\eta\in[\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,2\overline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}]
, and
\tau^{2}\in[\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}]
,

\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}p_{\chi^{2}}(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\mid K-p,\tau^{2})\right|\leq C_{0}(1+s^{2})(s^{2})^{(K-p)/2-1}\exp(-C_{0}^{-1}s^{2}).

Consequently,

\sup_{G\in\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{\mathsf{tr}}}\sup_{\eta\in[\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,2\overline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}]}\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}f_{G}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})\right|\leq C_{0}(1+s^{2})(s^{2})^{(K-p)/2-1}\exp(-C_{0}^{-1}s^{2}).

Choose
B(\varepsilon):=C_{2}\log(1/\varepsilon)
, for an appropriate constant
C_{2}>0
so that we have

\sup_{s^{2}\geq B(\varepsilon)}\sup_{G}\sup_{\eta}\left|\partial_{\eta}f_{G}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})\right|\leq\varepsilon/2.

Then any
\varepsilon
-cover of
\{f_{G}:G\in\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{\mathsf{tr}}\}
in the semi-metric
\|\cdot\|_{\infty,\partial,\eta}
may be constructed by covering only the truncated domain
s^{2}\in[0,B(\varepsilon)]
. By the above truncation,

\log N\!\left(\varepsilon,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{\mathsf{tr}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty,\partial,\eta}\right)\leq\log N\!\left(\varepsilon,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{\mathsf{tr}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty,\partial,[0,B(\varepsilon)],\eta}\right),

where

\|f_{G_{1}}-f_{G_{2}}\|_{\infty,\partial,[0,B(\varepsilon)],\eta}:=\sup_{s^{2}\in[0,B(\varepsilon)]}\sup_{\eta\in[\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,\,2\overline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}]}\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}f_{G_{1}}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})-\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}f_{G_{2}}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})\right|.

On
s^{2}\in[0,B(\varepsilon)]
,
\eta\in[\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,2\overline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}]
, and
\tau^{2}\in[\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}]
, the derivative kernel
(s^{2},\eta,\tau^{2})\mapsto\partial_{\eta}f_{\tau^{2}}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})
is smooth and uniformly bounded. Therefore, the same discretization-and-moment-matching construction used to prove Lemma S.4 of
22
applies to this derivative kernel on
[0,B(\varepsilon)]
, yielding a constant
C_{2}>0
depending only on
\nu,\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}},\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}
such that

\log N\!\left(\varepsilon,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{\mathsf{tr}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty,\partial,\eta}\right)\leq C_{2}\bigl(\log(1/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{2}.

Combining the density and derivative bounds completes the proof with
C:=C_{1}+C_{2}
.
∎

Lemma S3
.

Fix
s^{2}>0
and
|z|\geq\underline{z}
. Define

\displaystyle\widehat{g}_{\mathsf{tr}}(\eta;z,s^{2})

\displaystyle=C_{K,p}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{(t^{2})^{-\frac{K-p+1}{2}}\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}}{\sqrt{(K-p+1)t^{2}-(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)}}f_{\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},K-p+1}(t^{2})

(S17)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \times\mathds{1}\!\left\{t^{2}\geq\frac{(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)+z^{2}/(\nu^{2}\eta^{2})}{K-p+1}\right\}\,\mathrm{d}t^{2},

(S18)

for
\eta\in(0,\infty)
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\eta}\widehat{g}_{\mathsf{tr}}(\eta;z,s^{2})

\displaystyle=-C_{K,p}(K-p-2)\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{(t^{2})^{-\frac{K-p+1}{2}}\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{K-p}{2}-2}}{\sqrt{(K-p+1)t^{2}-(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)}}f_{\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},K-p+1}(t^{2})

(S19)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \times\mathds{1}\!\left\{t^{2}\geq\frac{(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)+z^{2}/(\nu^{2}\eta^{2})}{K-p+1}\right\}\,\mathrm{d}t^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-C_{K,p}(K-p)\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{(t^{2})^{-\frac{K-p+1}{2}}\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}}{\left((K-p+1)t^{2}-(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)\right)^{3/2}}f_{\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},K-p+1}(t^{2})

(S20)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \times\mathds{1}\!\left\{t^{2}\geq\frac{(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)+z^{2}/(\nu^{2}\eta^{2})}{K-p+1}\right\}\,\mathrm{d}t^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{K,p}\,f_{\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},K-p+1}\!\left(\frac{(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)+z^{2}/(\nu^{2}\eta^{2})}{K-p+1}\right)\times\frac{2\big((K-p)s^{2}+z^{2}/\nu^{2}\big)}{(K-p+1)\eta^{3}}

(S21)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \times\left(\frac{(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)+z^{2}/(\nu^{2}\eta^{2})}{K-p+1}\right)^{-\frac{K-p+1}{2}}\frac{\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}}{\sqrt{z^{2}/(\nu^{2}\eta^{2})}}

(S22)

Proof.

Write

\displaystyle\widehat{g}_{\mathsf{tr}}(\eta;z,s^{2})

\displaystyle=C_{K,p}\int_{\frac{(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)+z^{2}/(\nu^{2}\eta^{2})}{K-p+1}}^{\infty}\frac{(t^{2})^{-\,\frac{K-p+1}{2}}\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}}{\sqrt{(K-p+1)t^{2}-(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)}}f_{\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},K-p+1}(t^{2})\,\mathrm{d}t^{2}.

(S23)

We differentiate this integral with respect to
\eta
using Leibniz’ rule.

Set

\displaystyle\Phi(\eta,t^{2}):=\frac{(t^{2})^{-\,\frac{K-p+1}{2}}\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}}{\sqrt{(K-p+1)t^{2}-(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)}}f_{\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},K-p+1}(t^{2}),

(S24)

and

\displaystyle a(\eta):=\frac{(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)+z^{2}/(\nu^{2}\eta^{2})}{K-p+1}.

(S25)

Then

\displaystyle\widehat{g}_{\mathsf{tr}}(\eta;z,s^{2})=C_{K,p}\int_{a(\eta)}^{\infty}\Phi(\eta,t^{2})\,dt^{2}.

(S26)

Hence, by Leibniz’ rule,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\eta}\widehat{g}_{\mathsf{tr}}(\eta;z,s^{2})=C_{K,p}\left\{\int_{a(\eta)}^{\infty}\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}\Phi(\eta,t^{2})\,dt^{2}-a^{\prime}(\eta)\Phi(\eta,a(\eta))\right\}.

(S27)

We now compute the two terms separately.

First, since

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\eta}\left(\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)=-\frac{2s^{2}}{\eta^{3}},

(S28)

we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}=-\,(K-p-2)\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{3}}\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{K-p}{2}-2},

(S29)

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}\left((K-p+1)t^{2}-(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)\right)^{-1/2}=-\,(K-p)\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{3}}\left((K-p+1)t^{2}-(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)\right)^{-3/2}.

(S30)

Therefore

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}\Phi(\eta,t^{2})

\displaystyle=-\,(K-p-2)\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{3}}\frac{(t^{2})^{-\,\frac{K-p+1}{2}}\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{K-p}{2}-2}}{\sqrt{(K-p+1)t^{2}-(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)}}f_{\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},K-p+1}(t^{2})

\displaystyle\quad-\,(K-p)\frac{s^{2}}{\eta^{3}}\frac{(t^{2})^{-\,\frac{K-p+1}{2}}\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}}{\left((K-p+1)t^{2}-(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)\right)^{3/2}}f_{\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},K-p+1}(t^{2}).

(S31)

Substituting this into the integral term and rewriting the lower limit using the indicator gives the first two terms in the claimed formula.

Next, for the boundary term,

\displaystyle a^{\prime}(\eta)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{d\eta}\left[\frac{(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)+z^{2}/(\nu^{2}\eta^{2})}{K-p+1}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\big((K-p)s^{2}+z^{2}/\nu^{2}\big)}{(K-p+1)\eta^{3}}.

(S32)

Moreover, by direct substitution,

\displaystyle(K-p+1)a(\eta)-(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)=\frac{z^{2}}{\nu^{2}\eta^{2}},

(S33)

so that

\displaystyle\Phi(\eta,a(\eta))

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)+z^{2}/(\nu^{2}\eta^{2})}{K-p+1}\right)^{-\,\frac{K-p+1}{2}}\frac{\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}}{\sqrt{z^{2}/(\nu^{2}\eta^{2})}}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt\times f_{\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},K-p+1}\!\left(\frac{(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)+z^{2}/(\nu^{2}\eta^{2})}{K-p+1}\right).

(S34)

Hence

\displaystyle-\,a^{\prime}(\eta)\Phi(\eta,a(\eta))

(S35)

\displaystyle=f_{\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},K-p+1}\!\left(\frac{(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)+z^{2}/(\nu^{2}\eta^{2})}{K-p+1}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\times\frac{2\big((K-p)s^{2}+z^{2}/\nu^{2}\big)}{(K-p+1)\eta^{3}}\left(\frac{(K-p)\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)+z^{2}/(\nu^{2}\eta^{2})}{K-p+1}\right)^{-\,\frac{K-p+1}{2}}\frac{\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}}{\sqrt{z^{2}/(\nu^{2}\eta^{2})}}.

(S36)

Combining the integral term and the boundary term proves the result.
∎

Appendix H
Proofs of validity for oracle p-values

H.1
Proof of Lemma
5

Notice that if
\theta_{i}=0
, for any
t\geq 0
, by (
15
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{G}\left[|Z_{i}|\geq t\mid S^{2}_{i},M_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{G}\left[\mathbb{P}_{G}\left[|Z_{i}|\geq t\mid\theta_{i}=0,\tau^{2}_{i},S^{2}_{i},M_{i}\right]\mid S^{2}_{i},M_{i}\right]

(S37)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{G}\left[2\Phi(-t/\{\nu\xi_{0}(M_{i})\tau_{i}\})\mid S^{2}_{i},M_{i}\right]:=\mathrm{P}^{\text{reg}}(t,S^{2}_{i},M_{i};G).

(S38)

Also observe that, by (
17
) for all
i\in[n]

\displaystyle P^{\mathsf{tr}}_{i}=\mathrm{P}^{\text{reg}}(Z_{i},S^{2}_{i},M_{i};G).

(S39)

Therefore, the lemma follows using the probability integral transform conditioned on
S^{2}_{i}
and
M_{i}
.

H.2
Proof of Lemma
16

Under (
22
) and (
23
), the penultimate equality in (
S37
) does not hold. Nevertheless, one can directly condition on
V_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2}
and conclude that under the modified working model, for all
\theta_{i}=0
, or equivalently,
\omega_{i}=\theta_{i}/\xi_{\mathrm{mis}}(M_{i})=0
, we define
O_{i}:=Z_{i}/\xi_{\mathrm{mis}}(M_{i})
. Clearly,

O_{i}\mid\tau_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2}\sim\mathrm{N}(\omega_{i},\nu^{2}\tau_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2}),\qquad\mbox{for all $i\in[n]$.}

Therefore, we have for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{G_{\mathrm{mis}}}\left[|O_{i}|\geq t\mid V_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{G_{\mathrm{mis}}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{G_{\mathrm{mis}}}\left[|O_{i}|\geq t\mid\omega_{i}=0,\tau_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2},V_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2}\right]\mid V_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2}\right]

(S40)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{G_{\mathrm{mis}}}\left[2\Phi(-t/\{\nu\tau_{i,\mathrm{mis}}\})\mid V_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2}\right]:=\mathrm{P}^{\text{reg}}_{\mathrm{mis}}(t,V_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2};G_{\mathrm{mis}}).

(S41)

Again, by definition in (
17
), we have

P^{\mathsf{tr}}_{\mathrm{mis},i}:=\mathrm{P}^{\text{reg}}_{\mathrm{mis}}(O_{i},V_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2};G_{\mathrm{mis}}),

we can conclude using the probability integral transform that under (
22
) and (
23
)

P^{\mathsf{tr}}_{\mathrm{mis},i}\mid\omega_{i}=0,V_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(0,1),\qquad\mbox{almost surely, under the mixing measure $G_{\mathrm{mis}}$ on $\tau_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2}$.}

H.3
Proof of Lemma
17

Observe that under Assumption
1
, the triple
(Z_{i},S^{2}_{i},A_{i})
satisfy (
13
) and hence we can conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{H}\left[|Z_{i}|\geq t\mid S^{2}_{i},A_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{H}\left[\mathbb{P}_{H}\left[|Z_{i}|\geq t\mid\theta_{i}=0,\sigma^{2}_{i},\mu_{i},S^{2}_{i},A_{i}\right]\mid S^{2}_{i},A_{i}\right]

(S42)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{H}\left[2\Phi(-t/\{\nu\sigma_{i}\})\mid S^{2}_{i},A_{i}\right]:=\mathrm{P}^{\text{jt}}(t,S^{2}_{i},A_{i};H).

(S43)

Finally, using

P^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}=\mathrm{P}^{\text{jt}}(Z_{i},S^{2}_{i},A_{i};H),

and the probability integral transform, the lemma follows.

Appendix I
Results for parametric prior specification

In this section, we collect results when
G
in (
10
) or
H
in (
14
) belong to parametric families. This connects our results to the established results in
46
or
28
.

I.1
Parametric priors in
limma-trend

Define the following version of pooled variances corresponding to each unit under consideration.

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}^{2}_{i}:=\frac{(K-p)S^{2}_{i}+\kappa_{0}\xi^{2}_{0}(M_{i})s^{2}_{0}}{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}},\qquad\mbox{for $i\in[n]$.}

(S44)

Next, consider the following proposition.

Proposition S4
.

Let the distribution of
(Z,S^{2},M)
be distributed as (
15
) and (
9
). Then

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}\mid S^{2},M,\theta\sim t_{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}},

(S45)

where
\widetilde{T}:=(Z-\theta)/(\nu\widetilde{S})
and

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}^{2}:=\frac{(K-p)S^{2}+\kappa_{0}\xi^{2}_{0}(M)s^{2}_{0}}{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}

(S46)

Proof.

Let us first re-parametrize
\sigma^{2}
by the precision
\tau^{2}:=\sigma^{-2}
. Observe that, using (
8
) and (
9
), the joint density of
(Z,S^{2},\tau^{2})
given
M
can be written as

\displaystyle p(z,s^{2},\tau^{2}\mid M)

(S47)

\displaystyle\asymp(\tau^{2})^{\frac{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}+1}{2}-1}\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{(z-\theta)^{2}\tau^{2}}{\nu^{2}}+(K-p)s^{2}\tau^{2}+\kappa_{0}s^{2}_{0}\xi^{2}_{0}(M)\tau^{2}\right)\right)\cdot(s^{2})^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1},

(S48)

where the symbol
\asymp
implies equality up to multiplication by absolute constants depending on
K,p,\nu
and
\kappa_{0}
. Therefore, the joint distribution of
(Z,S^{2})
given
M
after marginalizing
\tau^{2}
is given by

\displaystyle p(z,s^{2}\mid M)

\displaystyle\asymp(s^{2})^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}\cdot\left(\frac{(z-\theta)^{2}}{\nu^{2}}+(K-p)s^{2}+\kappa_{0}s^{2}_{0}\xi^{2}_{0}(M)\right)^{-\frac{(\kappa_{0}+K-p+1)}{2}}.

(S49)

By the change of variable

\widetilde{t}:=\frac{z-\theta}{\nu}\cdot\left(\frac{(K-p)s^{2}+\kappa_{0}s^{2}_{0}\xi^{2}_{0}(M)}{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}\right)^{-1/2},

we can get that

\displaystyle p(\widetilde{t},s^{2}\mid M)

(S50)

\displaystyle\asymp(s^{2})^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}\times\left\{\left(\frac{\widetilde{t}^{2}}{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}+1\right)\left((K-p)s^{2}+\kappa_{0}s^{2}_{0}\xi^{2}_{0}(M)\right)\right\}^{-\frac{(\kappa_{0}+K-p+1)}{2}}

(S51)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \times\left(\frac{(K-p)s^{2}+\kappa_{0}s^{2}_{0}\xi^{2}_{0}(M)}{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}\right)^{1/2}

(S52)

\displaystyle\asymp\left(\widetilde{t}^{2}+(K-p)+\kappa_{0}\right)^{-\frac{(\kappa_{0}+K-p+1)}{2}}\times(s^{2})^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}\times\left((K-p)s^{2}+\kappa_{0}s^{2}_{0}\xi^{2}_{0}(M)\right)^{-\frac{(\kappa_{0}+K-p)}{2}}.

(S53)

This implies conditioned on
M
,
S^{2}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}\widetilde{T}
and
\widetilde{T}\sim t_{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}
.
∎

Next, consider the following lemma connecting the p-values
\{P^{\mathsf{tr}}_{i}\}
defined in (
17
) to the limma trend p-values from
28
.

Lemma S5
.

Assume that

\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}_{i}}\mid M_{i}\sim\frac{\chi^{2}_{\kappa_{0}}}{\kappa_{0}s^{2}_{0}\xi^{2}_{0}(M_{i})}.

Then the p-values
\{P^{\mathsf{tr}}_{i}\}
defined in (
17
) (or, equivalently (
11
) with the aforementioned prior) satisfy

P^{\mathsf{tr}}_{i}=P^{\texttt{ltrd}}_{i}=2\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{F}_{t,(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}(|Z_{i}/(\nu\widetilde{S}_{i})|),

where
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{F}_{t,d_{0}}
is the survival function of a
t
distribution with
d_{0}
degrees of freedom and
\widetilde{S}^{2}_{i}
defined in (
S44
). This aligns with the
limma-trend
p-values from
28
.

Proof.

Observe that
P^{\mathsf{tr}}_{i}
is obtained by plugging in
(Z_{i},S^{2}_{i},M_{i})
in the generic formula

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{\text{reg}}(z,s^{2},m;G)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{G}\left[|Z|\geq|z|\mid S^{2}=s,M=m\right],

(S54)

where
(Z,S^{2})
are distributed as specified in (
8
) and (
9
) with
\theta=0
.
Using Proposition
S4
, we get that if
G
is same as (
9
), then

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{\text{reg}}(z,s^{2},m;G)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{G}\left[|Z|\geq|z|\mid\theta=0,S^{2}=s,M=m\right]

(S55)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{G}\left[|Z/(\nu\widetilde{S})|\geq|z/(\nu\widetilde{s})|\mid\theta=0,S^{2}=s,M=m\right]

(S56)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{G}\left[|\widetilde{T}_{K-p+\kappa_{0}}|\geq|z/\nu\widetilde{s}|\right]

(S57)

\displaystyle=2\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{F}_{t,(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}(|z/(\nu\widetilde{s})|),

(S58)

where
\widetilde{S}^{2}:=\frac{(K-p)S^{2}+\kappa_{0}\xi^{2}_{0}(M)s^{2}_{0}}{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}
and
\widetilde{s}^{2}:=\frac{(K-p)s^{2}+\kappa_{0}\xi^{2}_{0}(M)s^{2}_{0}}{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}
. The above formula aligns with the
limma-trend
p-values from
28
.
∎

I.2
Connection between partially Bayes and fully Bayesian hypothesis testing

Next, we draw a connection with the partially Bayes approach of
limma-trend
with fully Bayes hypothesis testing based on posterior odds. In that direction, let us impose the spike-and-slab prior on the
\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{n}
, as follows:

\displaystyle\theta_{i}\mid\sigma^{2}_{i}\sim\pi\delta_{0}+(1-\pi)\mathrm{N}(0,v^{2}_{0}\sigma^{2}_{i}),

(S59)

in addition to (
8
) and (
9
). Consider the test statistics
\widetilde{T}_{j}=Z_{j}/(\nu\widetilde{S}_{j})
for
\widetilde{S}_{j}
defined in (
S44
). Then the posterior odds of the null hypothesis for testing the
j
-th hypothesis is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{j}:=\frac{p(\theta_{j}=0\mid\widetilde{T}_{j},S^{2}_{j},M_{j})}{p(\theta_{j}\neq 0\mid\widetilde{T}_{j},S^{2}_{j},M_{j})}.

(S60)

Consider the following proposition on the Bayes factors.

Proposition S6
.

Consider the prior on
\theta_{i}
given by (
S59
) along with the model from (
8
) and (
9
). Then the posterior odds
\mathcal{O}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{O}_{n}
from (
S60
) satisfies

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi}{(1-\pi)}\cdot\left(\frac{\nu^{2}}{v^{2}_{0}+\nu^{2}}\right)^{-1/2}\cdot\left(\frac{\widetilde{T}^{2}_{j}+(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}{\widetilde{T}^{2}_{j}(\nu^{2}/(\nu^{2}+v^{2}_{0}))+(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}\right)^{-\frac{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}+1}{2}},\quad\mbox{for all $j\in[n]$.}

(S61)

Furthermore,

\mathcal{O}_{j}\rightarrow\frac{\pi}{(1-\pi)}\cdot\left(\frac{\nu^{2}}{v^{2}_{0}+\nu^{2}}\right)^{-1/2}\exp\left(-\frac{\widetilde{T}^{2}_{j}}{2}\frac{v^{2}_{0}}{\nu^{2}+v^{2}_{0}}\right),\qquad\mbox{if $(K-p)+\kappa_{0}\rightarrow\infty$.}

Proof.

Observe that if
\theta_{i}=0
, then by Proposition
S4
,
\widetilde{T}_{j}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}(S^{2}_{j},M_{j})
and
\widetilde{T}_{j}\sim t_{K-p+\kappa_{0}}
. Next, observe that if
\theta_{j}\neq 0
, the only modification to (
8
) is that now

Z_{j}\mid\sigma^{2}_{j}\sim\mathrm{N}(0,(v^{2}_{0}+\nu^{2})\sigma^{2}_{j}).

Then the joint distribution of
(Z,S^{2},\tau^{2})
where
Z\mid\sigma^{2},M\sim\mathrm{N}(0,(v^{2}_{0}+\nu^{2})\tau^{-2})
and
(S^{2},\tau^{2},M)
distributed as (
8
) and (
9
), is given by

\displaystyle p(z,s^{2},\tau^{2}\mid M)

(S62)

\displaystyle\asymp(\tau^{2})^{\frac{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}+1}{2}-1}\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{z^{2}\tau^{2}}{(v^{2}_{0}+\nu^{2})}+(K-p)s^{2}\tau^{2}+\kappa_{0}s^{2}_{0}\xi^{2}_{0}(M)\tau^{2}\right)\right)\cdot(s^{2})^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}.

(S63)

After marginalizing over
\tau^{2}
, the joint distribution of
(Z,S^{2})
given
M
after marginalizing
\tau^{2}
is given by

\displaystyle p(z,s^{2}\mid M)

\displaystyle\asymp(s^{2})^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}\cdot\left(\frac{z^{2}}{2(v^{2}_{0}+\nu^{2})}+\frac{(K-p)s^{2}}{2}+\frac{\kappa_{0}s^{2}_{0}\xi^{2}_{0}(M)}{2}\right)^{-\frac{(\kappa_{0}+K-p+1)}{2}}.

(S64)

By the change of variable

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{t}:=\frac{z}{\sqrt{v^{2}_{0}+\nu^{2}}}\cdot\left(\frac{(K-p)s^{2}+\kappa_{0}s^{2}_{0}\xi^{2}_{0}(M)}{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}\right)^{-1/2},

we can get that

\displaystyle p(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{t},s^{2}\mid M)

(S65)

\displaystyle\asymp\left({\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{t}}^{2}+(K-p)+\kappa_{0}\right)^{-\frac{(\kappa_{0}+K-p+1)}{2}}\times(s^{2})^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}\times\left((K-p)s^{2}+\kappa_{0}s^{2}_{0}\xi^{2}_{0}(M)\right)^{-\frac{(\kappa_{0}+K-p)}{2}}.

(S66)

Therefore,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{T}:=\frac{Z}{\sqrt{v^{2}_{0}+\nu^{2}}}\cdot\left(\frac{(K-p)S^{2}+\kappa_{0}s^{2}_{0}\xi^{2}_{0}(M)}{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}\right)^{-1/2}\sim t_{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}
. This implies

\widetilde{T}_{j}\mid\theta_{j}\neq 0,M_{j}\sim\frac{\sqrt{v^{2}_{0}+\nu^{2}}}{\nu}t_{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}.

Also, the distribution of
S^{2}_{j}\mid\theta_{j}\neq 0,M_{j}
is same as the distribution of
S^{2}\mid\theta_{j}=0,M_{j}
for all
j\in[n]
. Therefore, the Bayes factor of the
j
-th unit is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi\,p(\widetilde{T}_{j}\mid\theta_{j}=0,M_{j})}{(1-\pi)\,p(\widetilde{T}_{j}\mid\theta_{j}\neq 0,M_{j})}

(S67)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi}{(1-\pi)}\cdot\left(\frac{\nu^{2}}{v^{2}_{0}+\nu^{2}}\right)^{-1/2}\cdot\left(\frac{\widetilde{T}^{2}_{j}+(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}{\widetilde{T}^{2}_{j}(\nu^{2}/(\nu^{2}+v^{2}_{0}))+(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}\right)^{-\frac{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}+1}{2}}.

(S68)

Then

\mathcal{O}_{j}\rightarrow\frac{\pi}{(1-\pi)}\cdot\left(\frac{\nu^{2}}{v^{2}_{0}+\nu^{2}}\right)^{-1/2}\exp\left(-\frac{\widetilde{T}^{2}_{j}}{2}\frac{v^{2}_{0}}{\nu^{2}+v^{2}_{0}}\right),\qquad\mbox{if $(K-p)+\kappa_{0}\rightarrow\infty$.}

∎

In the Bayesian literature it is common to use the logarithm of the Bayes factor
\log\mathcal{O}_{1},\ldots,\log O_{n}
to rank the significance of the hypotheses. In contrast, in the frequentist literature it is common to rank the significance of the hypotheses by the p-values. This principle is adopted in the Benjamini-Hochberg procedure. The above result Proposition
S6
and Lemma
S5
shows that the partially Bayes p-values from
limma-trend
results in the same ranking of the hypotheses (albeit, in the reverse order) as one would get for the fully Bayes framework.

I.3
Parametric priors in
Joint-NPMLE

Let us consider the following proposition when
H
in (
14
) takes a parametric form.

Proposition S7
.

Suppose the prior
H
for
(\mu_{i},\sigma_{i}^{2})
follows the following parametric hierarchical model:

\displaystyle\mu_{i}\mid\sigma_{i}^{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(a_{0},b_{0}\sigma_{i}^{2}),\quad\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}\sim\frac{1}{\kappa_{0}s_{0}^{2}}\chi_{\kappa_{0}}^{2},

(S69)

where
a_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
and
b_{0},\kappa_{0},s_{0}^{2}>0,
are known constant. Then,

\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}\mid A_{i}=a\sim\left(\kappa_{0}s_{0}^{2}+\frac{(a-a_{0})^{2}}{b_{0}+1/K}\right)^{-1}\chi^{2}_{\kappa_{0}+1}.

Furthermore,

\check{T}\mid S^{2},A,\theta\sim t_{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}+1},

where
\check{T}:=(Z-\theta)/(\nu\check{S})
, where
\check{S}^{2}:=\left\{\kappa_{0}s_{0}^{2}+(K-p)S^{2}+\frac{(A-a_{0})^{2}}{(b_{0}+1/K)}\right\}/(K-p+\kappa_{0}+1)
.
Moreover, the p-value
P_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}=2\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{F}_{t,(K-p)+\kappa_{0}+1}(|Z_{i}/(\nu\check{S}_{i})|)
, where

\check{S}^{2}_{i}=\frac{\kappa_{0}s_{0}^{2}+(K-p)S^{2}_{i}+\frac{(A_{i}-a_{0})^{2}}{(b_{0}+1/K)}}{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}+1},

and for any
\widetilde{d}>0
,
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{F}_{t,\widetilde{d}}
is the survival function of the t-distribution with
\widetilde{d}
degrees of freedom.

Proof.

Let
\tau^{2}=\sigma^{-2}
. Consider
(Z,S^{2},A,\mu,\sigma^{2})
distributed as (
13
) and (
14
) with
H
taking the form in (
S69
), we can conclude that

A\mid\tau^{2}\sim\mathrm{N}\left(a_{0},\frac{b_{0}+1/K}{\tau^{2}}\right).

Therefore, the joint likelihood of
(\tau^{2},A)
is given by

p(\tau^{2},a)\asymp\tau\exp\left\{-\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}\frac{(a-a_{0})^{2}}{b_{0}+1/K}\right\}\times\exp\left(-\frac{\kappa_{0}s^{2}_{0}\tau^{2}}{2}\right)\times(\tau^{2})^{\frac{\kappa_{0}}{2}-1}

Here the symbol
\asymp
implies the equality holds up to multiplication by an absolute constant depending
a_{0},b_{0},\kappa_{0},K,p
and
\nu
.
This implies by conjugacy

\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\mid A=a\sim\frac{1}{\,\kappa_{0}s_{0}^{2}+\frac{(a-a_{0})^{2}}{b_{0}+1/K}\,}\chi^{2}_{\kappa_{0}+1}.

Next, consider the joint density of
(Z,S^{2},A,\mu,\sigma^{2})
given as follows.

\displaystyle p(z,s^{2},a,\mu,\tau^{2})

\displaystyle\asymp\exp\left(-\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}\left\{\frac{(z-\theta)^{2}}{\nu^{2}}+(K-p)s^{2}+K(a-\mu)^{2}+\frac{(\mu-a_{0})^{2}}{b_{0}}+\kappa_{0}s^{2}_{0}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \times(\tau^{2})^{\frac{3+(K-p)+\kappa_{0}}{2}-1}(s^{2})^{\frac{(K-p)}{2}-1}.

We can marginalize the over
\mu
and
\tau^{2}
to obtain

\displaystyle p(z,s^{2},a)\asymp(s^{2})^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}\left(\frac{(z-\theta)^{2}}{\nu^{2}}+(K-p)s^{2}+\kappa_{0}s_{0}^{2}+\frac{(a-a_{0})^{2}}{b_{0}+1/K}\right)^{-\frac{(K-p)+\kappa_{0}+2}{2}}.

(S70)

Next, substituting

\check{t}:=\frac{z-\theta}{\nu}\cdot\left\{\frac{(K-p)s^{2}+\kappa_{0}s^{2}_{0}+\{(a-a_{0})^{2}/(b_{0}+K^{-1})\}}{K-p+\kappa_{0}+1}\right\}^{-1/2},

and proceeding as in the proof of Proposition
S4
, we can show that
\check{T}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}(S^{2},A)
and (
S45
) holds. The formula for p-values follows from

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{\text{jt}}(z,s^{2},a;H):=\mathbb{P}_{H}\left[|Z|\geq|z|\mid S^{2},A\right],

(S71)

by repeating the arguments in the proof of Lemma
S5
.
∎

Appendix J
Proof of asymptotic FDR control in Section
3.1

In this section, we prove the asymptotic FDR control result Theorem
22
under (
15
) and (
16
). We shall consistently assume Assumptions
6
and
7
.

J.1
Approximate NPMLE property of
\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}}

Observe that the solution
\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}}
of (
19
) is not an exact NPMLE under (
15
) and (
16
). Therefore, we do not automatically have

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log f_{\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},K-p}(S_{i}^{2}/\xi^{2}_{0}(M_{i}))\geq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log f_{G,K-p}(S_{i}^{2}/\xi^{2}_{0}(M_{i})).

However, we shall show the following result.

Lemma S8
.

There exist constants
C_{\mathsf{tr}}>0
and
n_{\mathsf{tr},1}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, depending only on
h_{1},h_{2},K,p,\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\mathsf{tr}},\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}
, such that the following hold with probability at least
1-o(n^{-2})

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{f_{\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},K-p}(S_{i}^{2}/\xi^{2}_{0}(M_{i}))}{f_{G,K-p}(S_{i}^{2}/\xi^{2}_{0}(M_{i}))}\geq e^{-n\,C_{\mathsf{tr}}\mathfrak{R}_{n,1}(\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}})},

(S72)

where for any
G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{tr}}

\mathfrak{R}_{n,1}(G^{\prime})=\Delta_{n}^{2}(\log n)^{2}+\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\Delta_{n}+\frac{(\log n)}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot|\log\Delta_{n}|^{h_{2}/2}\,\Delta^{1-h_{1}/2}_{n}+\mathcal{H}\!\left(f_{G^{\prime},K-p},f_{G,K-p}\right)\Delta_{n}.

Proof.

Recall that under the completely specified set-up (
15
) and (
16
), the conditional density of
S_{i}^{2}
given
M_{i}=m
under when the true trend is
\xi
and the mixing measure is
G^{\prime}
is proportional to
\eta^{-2}f_{G^{\prime},K-p}(S_{i}^{2}/\eta^{2})
, where
\eta=\xi(m)
. Let us define the log-likelihood (under
\xi
and
G^{\prime}
) as follows:

\ell(S_{i}^{2};\xi,G^{\prime},M_{i}):=\log f_{G^{\prime},K-p}(S_{i}^{2}/\xi^{2}(M_{i}))-2\log\xi(M_{i}).

Observe that in this framework, the following optimization program (as in (
19
))

\argmax_{G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{tr}}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log f_{G^{\prime},K-p}\!\left(S_{i}^{2}/\widehat{\xi}^{2}(M_{i})\right),

is equivalent to

\argmax_{G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{tr}}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(S_{i}^{2};\widehat{\xi},G^{\prime},M_{i}).

By the construction of the NPMLE
\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}}
,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(S_{i}^{2};\widehat{\xi},\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},M_{i})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(S_{i}^{2};\widehat{\xi},G,M_{i})\geq 0,\qquad\mbox{for $G$ defined in \eqref{eq:well-specified-limma-reg}.}

Our goal is to show that with probability greater than
1-o(n^{-2})
, there exists a constant
C_{\mathsf{tr}}>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(S_{i}^{2};\xi_{0},\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},M_{i})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(S_{i}^{2};\xi_{0},G,M_{i})-C_{\mathsf{tr}}\,\mathfrak{R}_{n,1}(\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}})

(S73)

where the function
\mathfrak{R}_{n,1}(\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}})
is defined in the lemma statement. This is also the approximate NPMLE property under the correctly scaled likelihood as defined in
25
. Define

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sub}_{n,1}=\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(S_{i}^{2};\xi_{0},\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},M_{i})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(S_{i}^{2};\widehat{\xi},\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},M_{i})\Big|

(S74)

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sub}_{n,2}=\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(S_{i}^{2};\xi_{0},G,M_{i})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(S_{i}^{2};\widehat{\xi},G,M_{i})\Big|.

(S75)

Then, we also have

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(S_{i}^{2};\xi_{0},\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},M_{i})\geq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(S_{i}^{2};\xi_{0},G,M_{i})-\mathrm{Sub}_{n,1}-\mathrm{Sub}_{n,2}.

So it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sub}_{n,1}+\mathrm{Sub}_{n,2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathsf{tr}}\,\mathfrak{R}_{n,1}(\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}}),

(S76)

with probability greater than
1-o(n^{-2})
.
Recall
V_{i}^{2}=S_{i}^{2}/\xi_{0}^{2}(M_{i})
. Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{tr}}=\{|M_{i}|\leq\mathrm{W}_{n},\;V_{i}^{2}\in[\widetilde{A}_{n},\widetilde{B}_{n}]\ \text{for all }i\in[n]\},

(S77)

where
\widetilde{A}_{n}=A_{n}/(2\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\mathsf{tr}})^{2}
and
\widetilde{B}_{n}=4B_{n}/\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}^{2}
, with
A_{n}=\underline{\kappa}n^{-6/(K-p)}
and
B_{n}=(3\overline{\kappa}\vee\widetilde{\kappa})\log n
as in Lemma
S1
. By Assumptions
6
-
7
, (
S10
), (
S11
), and a union bound,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{tr}}^{c})\leq 2n^{-2}
. Henceforth we work on
\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{tr}}
.

For any mixing distribution
G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{tr}}
, define

\displaystyle D(\eta,s^{2};G^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}\log f_{G^{\prime},K-p}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})-\frac{2}{\eta},\quad\mbox{and}

(S78)

\displaystyle H(\eta,s^{2};G^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\eta^{2}}\log f_{G^{\prime},K-p}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})+\frac{2}{\eta^{2}}.

(S79)

If we assume
\eta=\xi(m)
, where
\xi\in\mathcal{X}
(defined in Assumption
6
), then
\eta\in[\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,2\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}]
, and therefore, if
s^{2}\in[A_{n},B_{n}]
, the additional terms
-2/\eta
and
2/\eta^{2}
only change constants in the bounds from Lemma
S1
. In particular, Lemma
S1
(1) and Lemma
S1
(3) imply that if
s^{2}\in\left[(A_{n}\underline{M}^{2}_{\mathsf{tr}})/(4{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}}^{2}_{\mathsf{tr}}),(4B_{n}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}}^{2}_{\mathsf{tr}})/(\underline{M}^{2}_{\mathsf{tr}})\right]
and
\eta\in[\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,2\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}]
, then

|D(\eta,s^{2};G^{\prime})|\lesssim\log n,\quad\mbox{and}\quad|H(\eta,s^{2};G^{\prime})|\lesssim(\log n)^{2},

uniformly over
\eta\in[\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,2\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}]
,
s^{2}\in[A_{n},B_{n}]
, and
G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{tr}}
.

J.1.1
Bounding
\mathrm{Sub}_{n,1}

Applying a second-order Taylor expansion of
\ell(S_{i}^{2};\widehat{\xi},\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},M_{i})
around
\xi_{0}(M_{i})
gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(S_{i}^{2};\widehat{\xi},\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},M_{i})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(S_{i}^{2};\xi_{0},\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},M_{i})

(S80)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D(\xi_{0}(M_{i}),S_{i}^{2};\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}})\big(\widehat{\xi}(M_{i})-\xi_{0}(M_{i})\big)+\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}H(\eta_{t,i},S_{i}^{2};\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}})\big(\widehat{\xi}(M_{i})-\xi_{0}(M_{i})\big)^{2}

(S81)

for some
\eta_{t,i}=t_{i}\xi_{0}(M_{i})+(1-t_{i})\widehat{\xi}(M_{i})
with
t_{i}\in[0,1]
. Since
|H(\eta_{t,i},S_{i}^{2};\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}})|\lesssim(\log n)^{2}
on
\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{tr}}
, we obtain

\left\lvert\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}H(\eta_{t,i},S_{i}^{2};\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}})\big(\widehat{\xi}(M_{i})-\xi_{0}(M_{i})\big)^{2}\right\rvert\lesssim(\log n)^{2}\Delta_{n}^{2}.

It remains to bound the gradient term. Let us define

\mathrm{D}_{i}(v^{2},m;\xi_{0},G^{\prime}):=D(\xi_{0}(m),\xi_{0}^{2}(m)v^{2};G^{\prime}).

Then by definition of
V^{2}_{i}
, for any
G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{tr}}

D(\xi_{0}(M_{i}),S_{i}^{2};G^{\prime})=\mathrm{D}_{i}(V_{i}^{2},M_{i};\xi_{0},G^{\prime}).

In the subsequent analysis, we shall condition on
M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}
. Observe that under (
16
), the marginal density of
V^{2}=S^{2}/\xi^{2}_{0}(M)
is given by
f_{G,K-p}(v^{2})
(defined in (
18
)).
Therefore, define the truncated centering term by

\overline{\mathrm{D}}_{i}(\xi_{0},G^{\prime},M_{i}):=\int_{\widetilde{A}_{n}}^{\widetilde{B}_{n}}\mathrm{D}_{i}(v^{2},M_{i};\xi_{0},G^{\prime})f_{G,K-p}(v^{2})\,\mathrm{d}v^{2}.

We decompose

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{D}_{i}(V_{i}^{2},M_{i};\xi_{0},\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}})\big(\widehat{\xi}(M_{i})-\xi_{0}(M_{i})\big)=U_{1,n}+U_{2,n},

where

U_{1,n}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathrm{D}_{i}(V_{i}^{2},M_{i};\xi_{0},\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}})-\overline{\mathrm{D}}_{i}(\xi_{0},\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},M_{i})\big)\big(\widehat{\xi}(M_{i})-\xi_{0}(M_{i})\big)

and

U_{2,n}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{\mathrm{D}}_{i}(\xi_{0},\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},M_{i})\big(\widehat{\xi}(M_{i})-\xi_{0}(M_{i})\big).

Bounding
U_{1,n}
.

Recall the class of mixture densities
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{\mathsf{tr}}
from Lemma
S2
. Observe that, by definition
f_{\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},K-p}\in\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{\mathsf{tr}}
. Also, recall the pseudo-metric
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{joint}}
from the same lemma. Consider an
\varepsilon/4
-cover of the class
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{\mathsf{tr}}
in the pseudo-metric
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{joint}}
. Let the cover be
f_{G_{1},K-p},\ldots,f_{G_{N},K-p}
. By Lemma
S2
,

\log N\!\left(\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}_{\mathsf{tr}},\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{joint}},\varepsilon\right)\lesssim_{K,p,\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\mathsf{tr}},\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}}\bigl(\log(1/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{2}.

Let us focus on bounding the difference of scores between two mixture densities
f_{G_{1},K-p}
and
f_{G_{2},K-p}
, where
G_{1},G_{2}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{tr}}
. Observe that

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in[\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,\,2\overline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}]}\sup_{s^{2}\in[A_{n},B_{n}]}\left|\frac{\frac{df_{G_{1},K-p}}{d\eta}\!\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)}{f_{G_{1},K-p}\!\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)}-\frac{\frac{df_{G_{2},K-p}}{d\eta}\!\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)}{f_{G_{2},K-p}\!\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\eta\in[\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,\,2\overline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}]}\sup_{s^{2}\in[A_{n},B_{n}]}\left|\frac{\frac{df_{G_{1},K-p}}{d\eta}\!\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)-\frac{df_{G_{2},K-p}}{d\eta}\!\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)}{f_{G_{1},K-p}\!\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ +\sup_{\eta\in[\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,\,2\overline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}]}\sup_{s^{2}\in[A_{n},B_{n}]}\left|\frac{\frac{df_{G_{2},K-p}}{d\eta}\!\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)}{f_{G_{2},K-p}\!\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \times\sup_{\eta\in[\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,\,2\overline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}]}\sup_{s^{2}\in[A_{n},B_{n}]}\frac{\left|f_{G_{2},K-p}\!\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)-f_{G_{1},K-p}\!\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)\right|}{f_{G_{1},K-p}\!\left(s^{2}/\eta^{2}\right)}.

(S82)

Observe that for any
G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{tr}}
we have the following deterministic lower bound for
f_{G^{\prime},K-p}
on
[\widetilde{A}_{n},\widetilde{B}_{n}]
. Since
\operatorname{supp}(G^{\prime})\subset[\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}]
, for
x=s^{2}/\eta^{2}
,

f_{G^{\prime},K-p}(x)=\int p_{\chi^{2}}(x\mid K-p,\tau^{2})\,G^{\prime}(\mathrm{d}\tau^{2})\geq\inf_{\tau^{2}\in[\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}]}p_{\chi^{2}}(x\mid K-p,\tau^{2}),

where
p_{\chi^{2}}(x\mid K-p,\tau^{2})
is the
\tau^{2}\chi^{2}_{K-p}
density.
Using the explicit form of the scaled
\chi^{2}
density, for all
x\in[A_{n},B_{n}]
we have

\inf_{\tau^{2}\in[\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}]}p_{\chi^{2}}(x\mid K-p,\tau^{2})\gtrsim_{K,p,\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}}x^{\frac{K-p}{2}-1}\exp\!\left(-\frac{K-p}{2\overline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}}}x\right).

By the definition of
B_{n}
, it follows that there exists an absolute constant
C>0
(depending only on
K,p,\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{\kappa}_{n}
) such that

\inf_{\eta\in[\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,\,2\overline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}]}\inf_{s^{2}\in[A_{n},B_{n}]}f_{G^{\prime},K-p}(s^{2}/\eta^{2})\gtrsim n^{-C}.

Combining this with the score bound
|D(\eta,s^{2};G^{\prime})|\lesssim\log n
for
s^{2}\in[A_{n},B_{n}]
, we obtain from (
J.1.1
) that if
\|f_{G_{1},K-p}-f_{G_{2},K-p}\|_{\mathrm{joint}}\leq\varepsilon/4
, then

\sup_{x\in[\widetilde{A}_{n},\widetilde{B}_{n}]}\sup_{\eta\in[\underline{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}/2,2\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}_{\mathsf{tr}}]}\left|D(\eta,\eta^{2}x;G_{1})-D(\eta,\eta^{2}x;G_{2})\right|\lesssim(\log n)n^{2C}\varepsilon.

Taking
\varepsilon=n^{-2C-2}(\log n)^{-1}
, the same cover becomes an
n^{-2}
-cover for the score class. Furthermore

\displaystyle\log N\lesssim(\log n)^{2}.

(S83)

Since
\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}}\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{tr}}
, on
\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{tr}}
there exists
J\in[N]
such that uniformly in
i

\left|\mathrm{D}_{i}(V_{i}^{2},M_{i};\xi_{0},\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}})-\mathrm{D}_{i}(V_{i}^{2},M_{i};\xi_{0},G_{J})\right|\lesssim n^{-2}

and

\left|\overline{\mathrm{D}}_{i}(\xi_{0},\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},M_{i})-\overline{\mathrm{D}}_{i}(\xi_{0},G_{J},M_{i})\right|\lesssim n^{-2}.

Since
\|\widehat{\xi}-\xi_{0}\|_{\mathrm{W}_{n}}\leq\Delta_{n}
(by Assumption
7
), therefore

\Bigg|U_{1,n}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathrm{D}_{i}(V_{i}^{2},M_{i};\xi_{0},G_{J})-\overline{\mathrm{D}}_{i}(\xi_{0},G_{J},M_{i})\big)\big(\widehat{\xi}(M_{i})-\xi_{0}(M_{i})\big)\Bigg|\lesssim\frac{\Delta_{n}}{n^{2}}.

Since
\widehat{\xi}\in\mathcal{X}
, the forgoing inequality along with Assumption
7
implies

\displaystyle|U_{1,n}|\lesssim\mathfrak{V}_{n}+\frac{\Delta_{n}}{n^{2}}+\frac{\log n}{n^{2}},

(S84)

where

\mathfrak{V}_{n}:=\max_{j\in[N]}\max_{\xi\in\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathrm{D}_{i}(V_{i}^{2},M_{i};\xi_{0},G_{j})-\overline{\mathrm{D}}_{i}(\xi_{0},G_{j},M_{i})\big)\big(\xi(M_{i})-\xi_{0}(M_{i})\big)\right|,

and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}:=\left\{\xi\in\mathcal{X}:\|\xi-\xi_{0}\|_{\mathrm{W}_{n}}\leq\Delta_{n}\right\}
.
Let us define the processes
V_{n,j}(\xi):\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
for
j\in[N]
, as follows:

\displaystyle V_{n,j}(\xi):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathrm{D}_{i}(V_{i}^{2},M_{i};\xi_{0},G_{j})-\overline{\mathrm{D}}_{i}(\xi_{0},G_{j},M_{i})\big)\big(\xi(M_{i})-\xi_{0}(M_{i})\big).

(S85)

Observe that on
\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{tr}}
, each increment term in the processes
V_{n,j}(\xi)
is mean-zero conditioned on
M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}
. Therefore, for
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}
, using Lemma
S1
(1), we have that on
\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{tr}}
, conditioned on
M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}
and
\tau^{2}_{1},\ldots,\tau^{2}_{n}
,

\displaystyle\left|V_{n,j}(\xi_{1})-V_{n,j}(\xi_{2})\right|\lesssim\frac{(\log n)}{\sqrt{n}}\|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\|_{\mathrm{W}_{n}},

(S86)

where the semi-norm and
\mathrm{W}_{n}
are defined in Assumption
7
. Therefore, the processes
\{V_{n,j}(\xi)\}
has sub-Gaussian increments in terms of the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{W}_{n}}
on
\mathcal{A}_{\mathsf{tr}}
