\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c})+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\frac{|D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(H_{\widehat{j}})|}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(H_{\widehat{j}})}\cdot\mathds{1}(\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{E})\right]

(S368)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ +\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left|\frac{D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(H_{\widehat{j}})}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(H_{\widehat{j}})}-\frac{D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(\widehat{H})}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(\widehat{H})}\right|\cdot\mathds{1}(\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{E})\right]

(S369)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\frac{|D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(H_{\widehat{j}})|}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(H_{\widehat{j}})}\cdot\mathds{1}(\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{E})\right]

(S370)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ +\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left|\frac{D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(H_{\widehat{j}})}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(H_{\widehat{j}})}-\frac{D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(\widehat{H})}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(\widehat{H})}\right|\cdot\mathds{1}(\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{E})\right]

(S371)

Proceeding as in the proof of Lemma S10 of
22
, we can show that

\left|\frac{D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(H_{\widehat{j}})}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(H_{\widehat{j}})}-\frac{D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(\widehat{H})}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(\widehat{H})}\right|\lesssim\frac{\eta}{D_{i}(H)}.

Using the above relation and the definition of
\mathcal{E}
, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left|\frac{D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(H_{\widehat{j}})}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(H_{\widehat{j}})}-\frac{D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(\widehat{H})}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(\widehat{H})}\right|\cdot\mathds{1}(\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{E})\right]\lesssim\eta\penalty\ B_{\eta}A_{\eta}\lesssim_{K,p,M,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U},\underline{L}}\frac{(\log n)^{3/2}}{n}.

(S372)

Next, we consider

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\frac{\left|D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(H_{\widehat{j}})\right|}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(H_{\widehat{j}})}\,\mathds{1}(\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{E})\right],

which can be upper-bounded by

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\max_{j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\frac{\left|D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(H_{j})\right|}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(H_{j})}\,\mathds{1}(\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{E})\right]

We can decompose the above expression as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\max_{j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\left|\frac{D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(H_{j})}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(H_{j})}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(H_{j})}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(H_{j})}\right]\right|\right]

(S373)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ +\max_{j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(H_{j})}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(H_{j})}\right|\right]\right\}.

(S374)

Using the definition of
\mathcal{S}
along with the techniques used in the proof of Lemma S10 of
22
and Theorem
S16
, we can show that

\displaystyle\max_{j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(H_{j})}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(H_{j})}\right|\right]\right\}\lesssim_{K,p,M,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U},\underline{L}}\max_{j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\mathcal{H}\left(f_{H,K-p},f_{H_{j},K-p}\right)\lesssim\frac{(\log n)^{3/2}}{\sqrt{n}},

(S375)

and

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\max_{j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\left|\frac{D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(H_{\widehat{j}})}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(H_{\widehat{j}})}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{D_{i}(H)-D_{i}(H_{j})}{D_{i}(H)+D_{i}(H_{j})}\right]\right|\right]\lesssim_{K,p,M,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U},\underline{L}}\frac{(\log n)^{3/2}}{\sqrt{n}}.

Combining the above two displays with (
S367
) and (
S372
), the lemma follows.

L.5
Proof of Proposition
21

The proof of this proposition follows by combining the steps used to prove Proposition 15 of
22
and using Proposition
20
.

L.6
Proof of Theorem
22

Let
\widehat{F}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}
denote the empirical distribution function of the empirical Bayes
p
-values
\{\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. Define the data-driven Benjamini–Hochberg threshold

\displaystyle\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}}:=\sup\left\{t\in(0,1):\frac{t}{\widehat{F}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}(t)\vee n^{-1}}\leq\alpha\right\}.

(S376)

At nominal level
\alpha\in(0,1)
, the Benjamini–Hochberg procedure rejects hypothesis
j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
whenever
\widehat{P}_{j}^{\mathrm{jt}}\leq\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}}
.
Analogously, let
F_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}
denote the empirical distribution function of the oracle
p
-values
\{P^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, and define the corresponding oracle threshold

\displaystyle\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt},\mathrm{or}}:=\sup\left\{t\in(0,1):\frac{t}{F_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}(t)\vee n^{-1}}\leq\alpha\right\}.

(S377)

Define the associated empirical processes

\displaystyle V_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(t):=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathds{1}\{\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\leq t\},\qquad R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(t):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathds{1}\{\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\leq t\},

(S378)

where
\mathcal{H}_{0}
denotes the set of true null hypotheses and
\pi_{0}:=|\mathcal{H}_{0}|/n
.
Let
V_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}:=V_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})
and
R_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}:=R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})
. The false discovery rate (FDR) of the procedure is given by

\displaystyle\operatorname{FDR}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}:=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{V_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})}{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})\vee 1}\right].

(S379)

To establish Theorem
22
, we rely on the following auxiliary results.

Lemma S21

(Lemma S11 of
22
)
.

Fix
\zeta\in(\alpha,1)
and let
t\in(t_{0},t_{1})
, where
t_{0}
and
t_{1}
are defined in Assumption
11
. Suppose
\delta\in(0,t_{0}\wedge(t_{1}-t_{0}))
. Then, for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,

\mathds{1}(\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\leq t)-\mathds{1}(P_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\leq t+\delta)\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\left|\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\wedge\zeta-P_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\wedge\zeta\right|.

Similarly, we have for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,

\mathds{1}(\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\leq t)-\mathds{1}(P_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\leq t-\delta)\geq-\frac{1}{\delta}\left|\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\wedge\zeta-P_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\wedge\zeta\right|.

Lemma S22

(Lemma S12 of
22
(Bretagnolle–Dvoretzky–Kiefer–Wolfowitz))
.

Let
U_{i,n}\in[0,1]
,
i=1,\ldots,n
, be independent random variables (not necessarily identically distributed). Then, for every
\varepsilon\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathds{1}\{U_{i,n}\leq t\}-\mathbb{P}(U_{i,n}\leq t)\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)\leq 2\exp(-n\varepsilon^{2}).

In addition,

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathds{1}\{U_{i,n}\leq t\}-\mathbb{P}(U_{i,n}\leq t)\right|\right]\leq\sqrt{\frac{2e}{n}}.

Proof of Theorem
22
.

To prove Theorem
22
, we adopt a leave-one-out argument in the spirit of
22
.
For each
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, let
R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n,i}
denote the number of discoveries produced by the BH procedure applied to the leave-one-out p-values
\{\widehat{P}^{\mathrm{jt},-i}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}
, obtained from the modified data in which
Z_{i}
is replaced by

Z^{\infty}_{i}=\begin{cases}\infty&\text{if $Z_{i}\geq 0$,}\\
-\infty&\text{if $Z_{i}<0$.}\end{cases}

Note that the estimated prior
\widehat{H}
remains unchanged across these modified datasets, since
\widehat{H}
is computed only from the pairs
\{(S_{i}^{2},A_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
.
Using arguments analogous to the proof of Lemma C.1 of
43
, we have that if the
i
-th hypothesis is rejected, then
R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}=R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n,i}\geq 1
. Hence, for any
i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{H}\!\left[\frac{\mathds{1}\{\widehat{P}^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq\alpha R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}/n\}}{R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}\vee 1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{H}\!\left\{\mathbb{E}_{H}\!\left[\frac{\mathds{1}\{\widehat{P}^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq\alpha R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}/n\}}{R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}\vee 1}\penalty\ \Bigg|\penalty\ (S_{1}^{2},A_{1}),\ldots,(S_{n}^{2},A_{n}),R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n,i}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{H}\!\left\{\mathbb{E}_{H}\!\left[\frac{\mathds{1}\{\widehat{P}^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq\alpha R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n,i}/n\}}{R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n,i}}\penalty\ \Bigg|\penalty\ (S_{1}^{2},A_{1}),\ldots,(S_{n}^{2},A_{n}),R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n,i}\right]\right\},

(S380)

almost surely.

Let
\zeta:=\max\{3/4,(1+\alpha)/2\}
and
\underline{z}=\underline{L}^{1/2}z_{1-\zeta/2}
. Define

\Delta_{i}:=\sup_{z:|z|>\underline{z}}\left|\mathrm{P}^{\text{jt}}(z,S^{2}_{i},A_{i};\widehat{H})-\mathrm{P}^{\text{jt}}(z,S^{2}_{i},A_{i};H)\right|.

Repeating the arguments in the proof of Theorem 17 of
22
, we obtain that for all
i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
,

\mathbb{E}_{H}\!\left[\frac{\mathds{1}\{\widehat{P}^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq\alpha R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n,i}/n\}}{R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n,i}}\penalty\ \Bigg|\penalty\ (S_{1}^{2},A_{1}),\ldots,(S_{n}^{2},A_{n}),R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n,i}\right]\leq\frac{\alpha}{n}+\frac{\Delta_{i}}{R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n,i}},\qquad\text{a.s.}

Moreover, by construction of the leave-one-out p-values,
R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n,i}\geq R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}
for all
i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
.
Retracing the remainder of the proof of Theorem 17 of
22
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\operatorname{FDR}^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{H}\!\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\frac{\mathds{1}\{\widehat{P}^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq\alpha R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}/n\}}{R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}\vee 1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n_{0}}{n}\alpha+\mathbb{E}_{H}\!\left[\min\left\{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\frac{\Delta_{i}}{R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n,i}},1\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n_{0}}{n}\alpha+\mathbb{E}_{H}\!\left[\min\left\{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\frac{\Delta_{i}}{R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}},1\right\}\right].

(S381)

Consequently,

\left(\operatorname{FDR}^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}-\frac{n_{0}}{n}\alpha\right)_{+}\leq\mathbb{E}_{H}\!\left[\min\left\{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\frac{\Delta_{i}}{R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}},1\right\}\right].

Fix any sequence
\kappa_{n}\in(0,1)
such that

\mathbb{P}\!\left[R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}<n\kappa_{n}\right]\leq\eta_{n},\qquad\eta_{n}\in(0,1).

Then, using Proposition
20
together with the definition of
\zeta
and
\underline{z}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\operatorname{FDR}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}-\frac{n_{0}}{n}\alpha\right)_{+}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}[R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}<n\kappa_{n}]+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\frac{\Delta_{i}}{n\kappa_{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\underline{L},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U},M,K,p,\zeta}\eta_{n}+\frac{(\log n)^{13/4}}{\kappa_{n}\sqrt{n}}.

(S382)

It remains to choose
\kappa_{n}
and
\eta_{n}
.
Take any
t\in[t_{0},\alpha]
and
\delta\in(0,t_{0}\wedge(t_{1}-t_{0}))
, where
t_{0},t_{1}
are as in Assumption
11
.
By Lemma
S21
,

\displaystyle\frac{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(t)}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathds{1}\{\widehat{P}^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq t\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathds{1}\{P^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq t-\delta\}-\frac{1}{n\delta}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\wedge\zeta-P_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\wedge\zeta\right|.

(S383)

In particular, taking
t=t_{1}
yields

\displaystyle\frac{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(t_{1})}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathds{1}\{\widehat{P}^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq t_{1}\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathds{1}\{P^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq t_{1}-\delta\}-\frac{1}{n\delta}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\wedge\zeta-P_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\wedge\zeta\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\!\left[P^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq t_{1}-\delta\right]-\frac{1}{n\delta}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\wedge\zeta-P_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\wedge\zeta\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathds{1}\{P^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq t_{1}-\delta\}-\mathbb{P}\!\left[P^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq t_{1}-\delta\right]\right).

(S384)

By Assumption
11
, there exists

\alpha_{0,\mathrm{jt}}\in\left(\liminf_{n\to\infty}\inf_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\frac{1}{nt}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\!\left[P^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq t\right],\alpha\right)

such that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\!\left[P^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq t_{1}-\delta\right]\geq(t_{1}-\delta)\cdot\liminf_{n\to\infty}\inf_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\frac{1}{nt}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\!\left[P^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq t\right]\geq\frac{t_{1}-\delta}{\alpha_{0,\mathrm{jt}}},

(S385)

Equivalently, for any
\mathfrak{P}>0
there exists
n_{0}(\mathfrak{P})\geq 1
such that for all
n\geq n_{0}(\mathfrak{P})
,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\!\left[P^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq t_{1}-\delta\right]\geq\frac{t_{1}-\delta}{\alpha_{0,\mathrm{jt}}}+\mathfrak{P}.

Define the random error term

X_{n}:=\frac{1}{n\delta}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\wedge\zeta-P_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\wedge\zeta\right|+\sup_{t\in(0,1)}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathds{1}\{P^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq t\}-\mathbb{P}\!\left[P^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq t\right]\right)\right|.

By Markov’s inequality together with Proposition
20
and Lemma
S22
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\mathbb{P}\!\left[X_{n}>\frac{\delta}{4\alpha_{0,\mathrm{jt}}}\right]\leq\frac{4\alpha_{0,\mathrm{jt}}}{\delta}\,\mathbb{E}[X_{n}]\leq C\cdot\frac{4\alpha_{0,\mathrm{jt}}\,(\log n)^{13/4}}{\delta^{2}\sqrt{n}}.

On the event
\{X_{n}\leq\delta/(4\alpha_{0,\mathrm{jt}})\}
, combining with (
S384
) and the deterministic lower bound above gives, for all
n\geq n_{0}(\mathfrak{P})
,

\frac{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(t_{1})}{n}\geq\frac{t_{1}-\delta}{\alpha_{0,\mathrm{jt}}}+\mathfrak{P}-\frac{\delta}{4\alpha_{0,\mathrm{jt}}}.

Now set

\delta:=\frac{4t_{1}(\alpha-\alpha_{0,\mathrm{jt}})}{5\alpha},

so that
(t_{1}-\delta)/\alpha_{0,\mathrm{jt}}-\delta/(4\alpha_{0,\mathrm{jt}})=t_{1}/\alpha
and hence, for all
n\geq n_{0}(\mathfrak{P})
,

\frac{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(t_{1})}{n}\geq\frac{t_{1}}{\alpha}+\mathfrak{P}\geq\frac{t_{1}}{\alpha}\qquad\text{on the event }\left\{X_{n}\leq\frac{\delta}{4\,\alpha_{0,\mathrm{jt}}}\right\}.

By the self-consistency characterization of the BH threshold
\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}}
at level
\alpha
,

\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}}:=\sup\left\{t\in[0,1]:\frac{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(t)}{n}\geq\frac{t}{\alpha}\right\},

the inequality
R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(t_{1})/n\geq t_{1}/\alpha
implies
\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}}\geq t_{1}
. Since
R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(t)
is non-decreasing in
t
, it follows that

\frac{R^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}}{n}=\frac{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})}{n}\geq\frac{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(t_{1})}{n}\geq\frac{t_{1}}{\alpha},

on the same event. Consequently, taking

\kappa_{n}:=\frac{t_{1}}{\alpha},\qquad\eta_{n}:=C\cdot\frac{\alpha_{0,\mathrm{jt}}\,(\log n)^{13/4}}{\delta\sqrt{n}},

and substituting into (
L.6
), we obtain for all
n\geq n_{0}(\mathfrak{P})
,

\displaystyle\left(\operatorname{FDR}^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}-\frac{n_{0}}{n}\alpha\right)_{+}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\underline{L},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{U},M,K,p,\zeta}C\cdot\frac{4\alpha_{0,\mathrm{jt}}\,(\log n)^{13/4}}{\delta^{2}\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\alpha(\log n)^{13/4}}{t_{1}\sqrt{n}}.

(S386)

In particular, for any
\varrho>13/4
,

\sqrt{n}(\log n)^{-\varrho}\left(\operatorname{FDR}^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}-\frac{n_{0}}{n}\alpha\right)_{+}\longrightarrow 0.

∎

L.7
Power properties of the Benjamini Hochberg procedure using the estimated
Joint-NPMLE
p-values

To analyze the asymptotic power of the BH procedure, we impose a stronger assumption on the number of true null hypotheses

n_{0}:=\operatorname{card}(\mathcal{H}_{0})
(where
\mathcal{H}_{0}
denotes the subset of true null hypotheses) and on the mixing distribution, analogous to Assumption 8 of
22
.

Assumption S23
.

As
n\to\infty
, assume that
\frac{n_{0}}{n}\to\pi_{0}\in(0,1)
, and that

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{H}\!\left[P^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\leq t\right]\;\longrightarrow\;H_{\infty}^{\mathrm{jt}}(t),\qquad t\in(0,1),

for some distribution function
H_{\infty}^{\mathrm{jt}}
. Define

t_{\infty}^{\mathrm{jt}}:=\sup\left\{t\in(0,1):\frac{t}{H_{\infty}^{\mathrm{jt}}(t)}\leq\alpha\right\}.

We further assume that
t_{\infty}^{\mathrm{jt}}\in(0,\alpha)
and that the mapping

t\mapsto t/H_{\infty}^{\mathrm{jt}}(t)
is strictly increasing in a neighborhood of
t_{\infty}^{\mathrm{jt}}
.

The first condition ensures that the proportion of true null hypotheses is not asymptotically negligible, thereby yielding a nontrivial number of rejections. The second condition guarantees stability of the BH threshold by requiring local uniqueness of the solution to the defining inequality. More general versions of this assumption appear in Theorem 4 of
48
, where the null proportion
\pi_{0}
is estimated from the data and incorporated into the BH procedure. Related regularity conditions have also been studied by
18
and
12
.

We consider the following measures of power:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pow}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\frac{R_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}-V_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}}{n-n_{0}}\right],\qquad\mathrm{FNDR}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}:=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\frac{(\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}})^{c}\cap\mathcal{H}_{0}^{c}}{(n-R_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}})\vee 1}\right],

(S387)

where
\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}
denotes the rejection set produced by the BH procedure applied to the estimated p-values
\{\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\}
. The first measure of power was introduced in
18
, while the second was proposed by
19
. Both quantities were analyzed for the empirical Bayes
limma
procedure in
22
.

For comparison, we define the corresponding quantities for the BH procedure applied to the oracle p-values
\{P_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\}
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pow}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt},\mathrm{or}}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\frac{R_{n}^{\mathrm{jt},\mathrm{or}}-V_{n}^{\mathrm{jt},\mathrm{or}}}{n-n_{0}}\right],\qquad\mathrm{FNDR}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt},\mathrm{or}}:=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\frac{(\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt},\mathrm{or}})^{c}\cap\mathcal{H}_{0}^{c}}{(n-R_{n}^{\mathrm{jt},\mathrm{or}})\vee 1}\right],

(S388)

where
V_{n}^{\mathrm{jt},\mathrm{or}}
and
R_{n}^{\mathrm{jt},\mathrm{or}}
denote the numbers of false discoveries and total discoveries, respectively, produced by the BH procedure using oracle p-values, and
\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt},\mathrm{or}}
is the corresponding rejection set.

We now state our main result on asymptotic power equivalence.

Theorem S24
.

Under Assumptions
19
and
S23
,

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\left|\mathrm{Pow}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}-\mathrm{Pow}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt},\mathrm{or}}\right|=0,\qquad\limsup_{n\to\infty}\left|\mathrm{FNDR}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}-\mathrm{FNDR}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt},\mathrm{or}}\right|=0.

The proof follows by retracing the arguments used in the proof of Proposition 20 of
22
.

Appendix M
Limma-trend in the compound partially Bayes framework

M.1
Compound partial Bayes and FDR control in
Reg-NPMLE

In this section, instead of (
8
), we assume that the nuisance parameters
\tau^{2}_{1},\ldots,\tau^{2}_{n}
are fixed constants satisfying

\displaystyle\tau^{2}_{i}\in[\underline{L}_{\mathsf{tr}},\overline{U}_{\mathsf{tr}}]\quad\mbox{for all $i\in[n]$.}

(S389)

Furthermore, we also adopt the data generating model described in (
22
) with a working trend
\xi_{\mathrm{mis}}\in\mathcal{X}
such that the estimated trend
\widehat{\xi}
satisfies Assumption
13
with
\xi_{0}
replaced by
\xi_{\mathrm{mis}}
. In this framework, one can show that the NPMLE
\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}}
targets the empirical distribution of the nuisance parameters

\displaystyle G_{n}^{\mathsf{tr}}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{\tau_{i}^{2}}.

(S390)

Therefore, the target quantity approximated by
\widehat{P}^{\mathrm{mis}}_{i}
is

\displaystyle Q^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{i}=\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{n}2\,\Phi\!\left(-\frac{|O_{i}|}{\nu\tau_{j}}\right)\,p_{\chi^{2}}(V_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2}\mid K-p,\tau^{2}_{j})}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}p\!\left(V_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2}\,\Big|\,K-p,\tau^{2}_{j}\right)},\qquad\mbox{for $i\in[n]$.}

(S391)

One can show the following property of the sequence
\{Q^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{i}\}
.

Proposition S25
.

Under Assumption
S26
, the sequence
\{Q^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{i}\}
satisfies the following average significance controlling property.
Let

\mathcal{H}_{0}:=\{i\in[n]:\theta_{i}=0\}

denote the set of null indices. Then, for all
t\in(0,1)
,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathbb{P}_{\tau^{2}_{i}}\!\left[Q^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{i}\leq t\right]\leq t.

In other words,
\{Q^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{i}\}
forms a sequence of
compound p-values
as defined in
24
.

Proof.

Observe that

Q^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{i}:=\mathrm{P}^{\text{reg}}_{\mathrm{mis}}(O_{i},V_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2};G^{\mathsf{tr}}_{n}),

where for any
x\geq 0
,
\mathrm{P}^{\text{reg}}_{\mathrm{mis}}(x,V_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2};G^{\mathsf{tr}}_{n})
is defined through (
S40
). Again, by the proof technique in (
S40
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{\text{reg}}_{\mathrm{mis}}(O,V^{2}_{\mathrm{mis}};\mathrm{G})\mid\omega=0\sim\mathrm{Unif}(0,1),

(S392)

if
O\sim\mathrm{N}(\omega,\tau^{2}\nu^{2})
,
V^{2}_{\mathrm{mis}}\sim\tau^{2}\chi^{2}_{K-p}/(K-p)
and
\tau^{2}\sim\mathrm{G}
.
Define
O^{\prime}_{i}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\sim}\limits^{\text{iid}}}\,}\mathrm{N}(0,\tau^{2}_{i}\nu^{2})
. Observe that if
i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
, then
O^{\prime}_{i}\overset{d}{=}O_{i}\sim\mathrm{N}(0,\nu^{2}\tau^{2}_{i})
. Consequently, for any
x\in(0,1)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathbb{P}_{\tau^{2}_{i}}\!\left[Q^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{i}\leq t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathbb{P}_{\tau^{2}_{i}}\!\left[\mathrm{P}^{\text{reg}}_{\mathrm{mis}}(O_{i},V_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2};G^{\mathsf{tr}}_{n})\leq t\right]

(S393)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\tau^{2}_{i}}\!\left[\mathrm{P}^{\text{reg}}_{\mathrm{mis}}(O^{\prime}_{i},V_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2};G^{\mathsf{tr}}_{n})\leq t\right]

(S394)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\tau^{2}\sim G^{\mathsf{tr}}_{n}}\left[\mathrm{P}^{\text{reg}}_{\mathrm{mis}}(O^{\prime},V^{2};G^{\mathsf{tr}}_{n})\leq t\right],

(S395)

where
O^{\prime}\sim\mathrm{N}(0,\tau^{2}\nu^{2})
and
V^{2}\sim\tau^{2}\chi^{2}_{K-p}/(K-p)
. Using (
S392
), the lemma follows.
∎

Next, consider the following assumption on the data generating model.

Assumption S26
.

The tuples
(M_{i},Z_{i},V_{i,\mathrm{mis}}^{2})
are generated according to (
15
), and (
23
) for
K-p\geq 2
and are jointly independendent across
i\in[n]
. Furthermore, the random variables
M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}
satisfy

\mathbb{P}\!\left(\max_{i\in[n]}|M_{i}|>\mathrm{W}\sqrt{\log n}\right)\leq n^{-3}
for some
W>0
. Next, the latent parameters
\tau^{2}_{i}
are fixed constants satisfying (
S389
).

Under the foregoing assumption, one can show the following theorem, showing that the sequence
\{\widehat{P}^{\mathrm{mis}}_{i}\}
is a sequence of approximately compound p-values.

Proposition S27
.

Suppose Assumptions
13
and
S26
hold. Then there exists a constant
D_{\mathrm{cmp}}>0
(depending only on
\underline{L},\overline{U},M,K,p,\nu
, and
\zeta
) such that, for all
n\geq n_{\mathrm{cmp}}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,\zeta]}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathbb{P}_{\tau^{2}_{i}}\!\left[\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathsf{tr}}\leq t\right]-t\right\}

(S396)

\displaystyle\leq D_{\mathrm{cmp}}\cdot\max\left\{\Delta^{1/4}_{n}(\log n)^{5/4},\frac{(\log n)^{5/4}}{n^{1/8}}\Delta^{1/4}_{n},\frac{(\log n)}{n^{1/8}}\cdot|\log\Delta_{n}|^{h_{2}/8}\Delta^{\frac{1}{4}(1-h_{1}/2)}_{n},\frac{(\log n)^{5/4}}{n^{1/4}}\right\}.

(S397)

Proof.

To prove the above proposition under Assumptions
13
and
S26
, one can retrace the arguments in the proof of Lemma
S11
and Theorem 9* of
22
to show that

\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{H}^{2}(f_{\widehat{G}_{\mathsf{tr}},K-p},f_{G^{\mathsf{tr}}_{n},K-p})\geq\widetilde{D}_{\mathrm{cmp}}\lambda^{2}_{n,2}\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}}+e^{-c_{0}(\log n)^{2}},

where
\widetilde{D}_{\mathrm{cmp}}>0
is an absolute constant and
\lambda^{2}_{n,2}
is defined in Lemma
S11
. This result can, in turn, be combined with the techniques adopted to prove Proposition
S12
and Theorem 13* to show that for any
\zeta\in(1/2,1)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[|Q^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{i}\wedge\zeta-\widehat{P}^{\mathsf{tr}}_{i}\wedge\zeta|\right]\lesssim\mathfrak{L}_{n,\mathrm{mis}},

(S398)

where
\mathfrak{L}_{n,\mathrm{mis}}
is defined in the statement of Proposition
S12
.
Now, retracing the arguments used to prove Proposition 15* of
22
and using the foregoing relation, we can conclude the lemma.
∎

Furthermore, one can use the estimated compound p-values
\{\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathsf{tr}}\}
in the Benjamini-Hochberg procedure to test
H_{i}:\theta_{i}=0
with the level of significance
\alpha\in(0,1)
. In the following theorem, we show that the asymptotic FDR remains controlled at the desired nominal level of significance.

Theorem S28
.

Suppose Assumptions
13
and
S26
hold. Fix
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Assume that the true p-values
\{Q^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{i}\}
are critically dense at
\alpha
. Furthermore, also assume the conditions in Proposition
S27
. Then

\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\operatorname{FDR}^{\mathrm{mis}}_{n}\;\leq\alpha,

where
\operatorname{FDR}^{\mathrm{mis}}_{n}:=\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\mathrm{mis}}_{n}/(R^{\mathrm{mis}}_{n}\vee 1)\right]
.

The proof of the theorem follows using (
S396
), (
S398
), and retracing the arguments of the proof of Theorem 17* of
22
.

M.2
Compound partial Bayes and FDR control in
Joint-NPMLE

Under this framework, we relax the bivariate prior specification on the nuisance parameters in (
14
) and instead assume that
(\mu_{1},\sigma^{2}_{1}),\ldots,(\mu_{n},\sigma^{2}_{n})
are fixed constants satisfying

\displaystyle\mu_{i}\in[-M,M],\quad\mbox{and}\quad\sigma^{2}_{i}\in[\underline{L},\overline{U}]\quad\mbox{for all $i\in[n]$.}

(S399)

As shown in Section
M
, one can still use
\{\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\}
computed by plugging in
\widehat{H}
(defined in (
25
)) in (
24
) to test the hypotheses
H_{0}:\theta_{i}=0
. As in Section
M
, within this framework, one can show that the sequence the
\{\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\}
approximates the following quantities:

\displaystyle P_{i}^{\mathrm{cmp}}:=\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{n}2\Phi\!\left(-|Z_{i}|/(\nu\sigma_{j})\right)\,p_{K-p}(S_{i}^{2},A_{i}\mid\mu_{j},\sigma_{j}^{2})}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}p_{K-p}(S_{i}^{2},A_{i}\mid\mu_{j},\sigma_{j}^{2})},

(S400)

where
p(s^{2},a\mid\mu,\sigma^{2})
is defined in (
26
). The sequence
\{P_{i}^{\mathrm{cmp}}\}
satisfies the following average significance controlling property.

Lemma S29
.

Consider the data-generating mechanism in (
13
), and (
14
). Also consider the p-values
\{P_{i}^{\mathrm{cmp}}\}
defined in (
S400
). Recall

\mathcal{H}_{0}:=\{i\in[n]:\theta_{i}=0\},

the set of null indices. Then, under Assumption
1
, for all
t\in(0,1)
,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathbb{P}_{(\sigma_{i}^{2},\mu_{i})}\!\left[P_{i}^{\mathrm{cmp}}\leq t\right]\leq t.

In other words,
\{P^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{i}\}
forms a sequence of
compound p-values
as defined in
24
.

The proof of this lemma follows by retracing the arguments in the proof of Theorem 21 of
22
.
Next, consider the following assumption on the data generating model.

Assumption S30
.

The tuples
(Z_{i},S^{2}_{i},A_{i})
are generated according to (
13
) for
K-p\geq 2
and are jointly independendent across
i\in[n]
. The latent parameters
(\mu_{i},\sigma^{2}_{i})
are fixed constants satisfying (
S399
).

Furthermore, one can show that under (
S399
), the following proposition holds.

Proposition S31
.

If Assumption
S30
holds, there exists a constant
C_{\mathrm{cmp}}>0
(depending only on
\underline{L},\overline{U},M,K,p,\nu
, and
\zeta
) such that, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
,

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\sup_{t\in(0,\alpha)}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathds{1}\{\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\leq t\}-t\right|\right]\leq C_{\mathrm{cmp}}\,\frac{(\log n)^{13/8}}{n^{1/4}}.

Proof.

To prove the above theorem, one first shows that there exist constants
\mathfrak{F}_{0}>0
and
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, depending only on
K,p,M,\underline{L},\overline{U}
and
\mathfrak{d}_{0}
, such that for all
n\geq n_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{(\mu,\sigma)}\!\left[\mathcal{H}^{2}\!\left(f_{\widehat{H},K-p},f_{H_{n},K-p}\right)\geq\mathfrak{F}_{0}\frac{(\log n)^{3}}{n}\right]\leq\exp\!\left(-\mathfrak{d}_{0}\log n\right).

(S401)

Here the probability is taken with respect to the joint distribution of
\{(S_{i}^{2},A_{i}):i\in[n]\}
, where for each
i
,
(S_{i}^{2},A_{i})
is generated according to (
4
) and (
12
) with parameters
(\mu_{i},\sigma_{i}^{2})
. The proof of the foregoing relation follows by using the definition of the NPMLE and modifying the arguments in the proof of Theorem
S16
as in the proof of Theorem 9* of
22
. Next, one can modify the arguments in the proof of Theorem
20
along the lines of the proof of Theorem 13* of
22
to show that for any
\zeta\in(1/2,1)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{P}^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\wedge\zeta-P^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{i}\wedge\zeta\right|\right]\lesssim\frac{(\log n)^{13/4}}{\sqrt{n}}.

(S402)

Furthermore, one can also use (
S401
), Lemma
S29
and retrace the arguments in the proof of Proposition 15* of
22
to conclude the proposition.
∎

Now, consider a BH procedure to test
H_{i}:\theta_{i}=0
using the NPMLE based
Joint-NPMLE
p-values
\{\widehat{P}^{\mathrm{jt}}_{i}\}
. Let
\operatorname{FDR}^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}
be the false discovery rate of the resulting procedure.

Theorem S32
.

If Assumption
S30
holds and the true p-values
\{P^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{i}\}
are critically dense at
\alpha
as defined in Assumption
11
, then under (
S399
), we have the following.

\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\operatorname{FDR}^{\mathrm{jt}}_{n}\;\leq\alpha.

Proof.

Since the conditional validity property analogous to (
28
) does not hold for the compound p-values
\{P_{i}^{\mathrm{cmp}}\}
, we cannot directly follow the proof of Theorem
22
. Instead, we decompose the false discovery proportion. For any deterministic sequence
\kappa_{n}>0
,

\displaystyle\frac{V_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})}{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})\vee 1}\leq\mathds{1}\{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})<n\kappa_{n}\}+\frac{V_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})}{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})}\mathds{1}\{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})\geq n\kappa_{n}\}.

(S403)

Taking expectations yields

\displaystyle\operatorname{FDR}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}\leq\mathbb{P}\!\left(R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})<n\kappa_{n}\right)+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\frac{V_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})}{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})}\mathds{1}\{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})\geq n\kappa_{n}\}\right].

(S404)

Next observe that

\displaystyle\frac{V_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})}{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})}=\frac{n\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}}}{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})}+\frac{n}{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathds{1}\{\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\leq\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}}\}-\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}}\right\}.

(S405)

On the event
\{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})\geq n\kappa_{n}\}
, the definition of the BH threshold implies
(n\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})/R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})\leq\alpha
, and therefore

\displaystyle\frac{V_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})}{R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})}\leq\alpha+\frac{1}{\kappa_{n}}\sup_{t\in(0,\alpha)}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathds{1}\{\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\leq t\}-t\right|.

(S406)

Consequently,

\displaystyle\operatorname{FDR}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}\leq\mathbb{P}\!\left(R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})<n\kappa_{n}\right)+\alpha+\frac{1}{\kappa_{n}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\sup_{t\in(0,\alpha)}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathds{1}\{\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\leq t\}-t\right|\right].

(S407)

By Proposition
S31
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\sup_{t\in(0,\alpha)}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathds{1}\{\widehat{P}_{i}^{\mathrm{jt}}\leq t\}-t\right|\right]\lesssim_{K,p,\underline{L},\overline{U},\nu,\alpha}\frac{(\log n)^{13/8}}{n^{1/4}}.

(S408)

Since the oracle compound p-values are critically dense at
\alpha
, retracing the final step in the proof of Theorem
22
shows that for
\kappa_{n}=5\alpha/4(\alpha-\alpha_{0})
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\!\left(R_{n,\mathrm{jt}}(\widehat{t}_{\mathrm{jt}})<n\kappa_{n}\right)=0.

(S409)

Combining the above bounds yields

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\operatorname{FDR}_{n}^{\mathrm{jt}}\leq\alpha.

This completes the proof.
∎
